<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
Let $Y$ be a stationary point for the random dynamical system generated by \eqref{MAIN0}. Then the dynamics admit local stable manifolds associated with the negative Lyapunov spectrum. If the Lyapunov spectrum contains positive, respectively zero, exponents, then local unstable, respectively center, manifolds also exist.
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
\label{TGsasa}
		In the concrete model considered in this manuscript, the realization map $\Pi$ is given by
		\begin{align*}
			& \Pi_z(\textcolor{red}{\bullet}) = \bullet, \quad
			\Pi_z(\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}) = X^{i} - X^{i}(z), \quad
			\Pi_z( 
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
) =  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\bullet$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 -  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\bullet$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
(z), \\
			&
			\Pi_z(\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}) = 1, \quad
			\Pi_z(\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}\textcolor{red}{\bullet}) = \Pi_z(\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}})\,\bullet .
		\end{align*}
		Here $ \bullet \in \mathcal{D}'(\mathbb{R}^{d+1}) $ and $  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\bullet$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ \in C^{1-\kappa}_{(2,1)}$ are spatially periodic. The definition of $\Pi_z(( 
	\mathbin{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.35ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.4em$\textcolor{red}{\mathord{\rotatebox[origin=c]{60}{$/$}}}$ 
				\kern-0.45em\raisebox{-1.ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
))$ is more subtle and is naively given by
		\[
		( 
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\bullet$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
- 
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\bullet$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
(z))\bullet-\infty
		\]
		in the renormalisation sense.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- assumption-1: body_md -->
\label{UASassasa}
		We assume that
		\begin{enumerate}
			\item  
			We consider the following $2\pi$-periodic problem:
			\begin{align}\label{MAIN}
				\begin{cases}
					(\partial_t-\Delta)u
					=
					\mu u+\Sigma(u)\,\xi,
					& t>s,
					\\
					u(s,\cdot)=u_0,
					& u_0\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d).
				\end{cases}
			\end{align}
			Here
			\[
			\mathbb{T}^d=(\mathbb{R}/2\pi\mathbb{Z})^d,
			\]
			$\xi\in\mathcal{C}^{-1-\kappa}$ with
			$0<\kappa<\frac13$, $\mu\in\mathbb{R}$, and
			$\Sigma\in C_b^2(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R})$.
			
			\item We continue to work with the notation and assumptions introduced in
			the previous part. In particular, for $0<\kappa<\frac13$, we set
			\begin{align}
				\begin{split}
					1+\kappa &< \gamma_1 < 2-2\kappa,\\
					\gamma_0 &:= \gamma_1-1-\kappa.
				\end{split}
			\end{align}
			
			\item We fix a model $\mathcal{Z}=(\Pi,\Gamma)$.
		\end{enumerate}
<!-- end assumption-1 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{stat}
		Let $\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}\in\mathcal{D}^{\gamma_1,0}_{P_{s},0}|_{O_{T+s}^s}$, then
		\begin{align*}
			\lvert\!\lVert \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}\tilde{\star}\textcolor{red}{\bullet} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_0,-1-\kappa,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}
			=
			\lvert\!\lVert \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}.
		\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
By definition, for $z,w\in \mathbb{R}^{d+1}\setminus P_{s}$, we have
		\begin{align*}
			\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}\tilde{\star}\textcolor{red}{\bullet}(z)
			&:=
			{\mathcal{U}}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(z)\textcolor{red}{\bullet}
			+
			{\mathcal{U}}^{\small{ 
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
}}(z) 
	\mathbin{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.35ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.4em$\textcolor{red}{\mathord{\rotatebox[origin=c]{60}{$/$}}}$ 
				\kern-0.45em\raisebox{-1.ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
			} 
		} 
	} 

			+
			\sum_{i=1}^{d}
			{\mathcal{U}}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}(z)\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}\textcolor{red}{\bullet},
		\end{align*}
		and
		\begin{align*}
			\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}\tilde{\star}\textcolor{red}{\bullet}(z)
			-
			\Gamma_{z,w}
			\left(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}\tilde{\star}\textcolor{red}{\bullet}(w)\right)
			=
			\big(
			\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(z)
			-
			\Gamma_{z,w}\left(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w)\right)
			\big)\tilde{\star}\textcolor{red}{\bullet}.
		\end{align*}
		The claim now follows directly from the definition.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
\label{BOUNDED}
		Assume that $F\in C_b^2(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R})$ and
		$\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}\in
		\mathcal{D}^{\gamma_1,0}_{P_s,0}\big|_{O_{T+s}}$.
		Then, by standard estimates, for every $T\in(0,1]$,
		\[
		\lvert\!\lVert 
			\mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}F(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})\tilde{\star}\textcolor{red}{\bullet}
		 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_0,-1-\kappa,O_{s+T}^{\,s}}^{(s)}
		=
		\lvert\!\lVert 
			\mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}F(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})
		 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{s+T}^{\,s}}^{(s)}
		\lesssim
		1+\big([ \  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ ]_{O_{T+s}^{\,s}}\big)^2
		+
		\bigg(
		\lvert\!\lVert 
			\mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}
		 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{s+T}^{\,s}}^{(s)}
		\bigg)^2.
		\]
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{dfn}
		Let $s\in\mathbb{R}$, $T>0$, and $u_0\in L^\infty(\mathbb{T}^d)$.
		Suppose that
		\[
		\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}
		\in
		\mathcal{D}^{\gamma_1,0}_{P_s,0}\big|_{O^s_{s+T}}
		\]
		is the fixed point of the operator $\mathcal{Q}_T^s$
		defined in \eqref{FIXED}. For $z=(t,x)\in O^s_{s+T}$, write
		\[
		\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(z)
		=
		\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(z)\,\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}
		+
		\mathcal{U}^{ 
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
}_{u_0}(z)\, 
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 

		+
		\sum_{i=1}^{d}
		\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}_{u_0}(z)\,\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}} .
		\]
At the initial time, we set
\[
\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(s,\cdot):=u_0
\quad\text{in }L^\infty(\mathbb{T}^d).
\]
		We then say that $\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}$ solves
		\begin{align}\label{MAIN_1}
			\begin{cases}
				(\partial_t-\Delta)\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}
				=
				\mu\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}
				+
				\Sigma(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})\tilde{\star}\textcolor{red}{\bullet},
				& t>s,\\
				\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(s,\cdot)=u_0,
				& t=s,
			\end{cases}
		\end{align}
		on $[s,s+T]$ with initial condition $u_0$ and with respect to the model
		$\mathcal{Z}$.
		The corresponding actual solution is defined by
		\[
		\mathcal{R}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0})(z)
		=
		\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(z),
		\qquad
		z\in(s,s+T]\times\mathbb{R}^d .
		\]
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
From \eqref{A1}, we can see that for each
		$z\in(s,T+s]\times\mathbb{R}^d$,
		\begin{align*}
			\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(z)
			=
			\mathcal{U}_{u_0}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(z)\,\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}
			+
			\Sigma\big(\mathcal{U}_{u_0}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(z)\big)\, 
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 

			+
			\sum_{i=1}^{d}
			\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}_{u_0}(z)\,\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}.
		\end{align*}
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
For each $s\in\mathbb{R}$, there exists $T>0$ such that
		\eqref{MAIN_1} admits a unique local solution on $O^s_{s+T}$.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{global}
		Let $T\in(0,1]$ and $	\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}\in
		\mathcal{D}^{\gamma_1,0}_{P_s,0}\big|_{O^s_{s+T}}.$
		Suppose that there exist $0<\delta_0\leq T$ and a constant
		$\Lambda>0$ such that
		\begin{align}\label{ASASa}
			\forall\, s\leq b<c\leq s+T,\quad c-b\leq\delta_0:
			\qquad
			\lvert\!\lVert 
				\mathbf{R}^{+}_{b}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}
			 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_c^{b}}^{(b)}
			\leq \Lambda .
		\end{align}
		Then
		\begin{align*}
			\lvert\!\lVert 
				\mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}
			 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O^s_{s+T}}^{(s)}
			\lesssim
			\left(\frac{1}{\delta_0}\right)^{\frac{\gamma_1+2}{2}}
			\big(\|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O^s_{s+T}}}\big)^2
			\Lambda .
		\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
For a better flow of the proof, we divide it into several steps.
		
		\textbf{Step 1.}\label{Step1}
		Let
		\begin{align*}
			\mathcal{A}^{+} := \{ \large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}},  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
, \large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}} : i = 1,\ldots,d \}.
		\end{align*}
		The large bulk of the argument is devoted to estimating
		\begin{align}\label{Kmasas}
			\sup_{\substack{a\in\mathcal{A}^+}} \sup_{\substack{ z,w\in O_{T+s}^{s},\ z\neq w,\\ d(z,w)\leq\Vert z,w\Vert_{P_s} }}
			\frac{
				\Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1}
				\, \Vert \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(z) - \Gamma_{z,w}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w) \Vert_{a}
			}{
				d(z,w)^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}
			}.
		\end{align}
		Let $z=(t_0,y_0)$ and $w=(t_1,y_1)$ be elements of
		$O_{s+T}^{s} = (s, s+T] \times \mathbb{R}^d$ such that
		$d(z,w)\leq \Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}$.
		Then
		\begin{align}\label{2369}
			\max \Big\{ \sqrt{|t_1 - t_0|}, \Vert y_1 - y_0\Vert_{\mathbb{T}^d} \Big\}
			\leq \Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}
			= \min\bigl\{ \sqrt{t_0 - s}, \sqrt{t_1 - s} \bigr\}.
		\end{align}
		For simplicity during the proof, we assume that
		\begin{enumerate}[label=\textbf{(*)}]
			\item \label{cond:A}
			\[
			\|z,w\|_{P_s} = \|z\|_{P_s} = \sqrt{t_0 - s}.
			\]
		\end{enumerate}
		The argument for the other case is similar.
		Let $\tilde{z}=(t_1,y_0)$. Then
		\begin{align}\label{BNasr}
			d(z,\tilde{z}) \leq \Vert z,\tilde{z}\Vert_{P_s} = \Vert z\Vert_{P_s}
			\leq \Vert\tilde{z},w\Vert_{P_s}=\Vert\tilde{z}\Vert_{P_s}
			\qquad \text{and} \qquad
			\max\lbrace	d(z,\tilde{z}), d(\tilde{z},w)\rbrace \leq d(z,w).
		\end{align}
		\begin{center}
			\begin{tikzpicture}[scale=.6]
				
				        
				\definecolor{mypink}{RGB}{220,20,120}
				\definecolor{myblue}{RGB}{30,50,220}
				\definecolor{mygreen}{RGB}{50,180,50}
				
				           
				\draw[line width=1.1pt] (0,0) rectangle (8,5);
				
				                         
				\draw[dashed] (0,1.6) -- (8,1.6);
				\draw[dashed] (0,4.0) -- (8,4.0);
				
				                       
				\draw[dashed] (2,0) -- (2,1.6);
				\draw[dashed] (6,0) -- (6,1.6);
				
				           
				\draw[line width=1.8pt,myblue] (2,1.6) -- (6,1.6);
				\draw[line width=1.8pt,myblue] (6,1.6) -- (6,4.0);
				
				              
				\fill[mypink!0!mygreen,mygreen] (2,1.6) circle (2pt);
				\fill[mypink!0!mygreen,mygreen] (6,1.6) circle (2pt);
				\fill[mypink!0!mygreen,mygreen] (6,4.0) circle (2pt);
				
				               
				\node[above left] at (2,1.6) {$z$};
				\node[right] at (6,2) {$\tilde z$};
				\node[above] at (6,4.0) {$w$};
				
				                  
				\node[left] at (-0.2,2.8) {\textcolor{red}{$\mathbb{T}^d$}};
				
				\node[left] at (0,1.6) {$y_0$};
				\node[left] at (0,4.0) {$y_1$};
				
				               
				\node[below] at (2,0) {$t_0$};
				\node[below] at (6,0) {$t_1$};
				
				\node[below left] at (0,0) {\textcolor{mypink}{$s$}};
				\node[below right] at (8,0) {\textcolor{mypink}{$s+T$}};
				
			\end{tikzpicture}
		\end{center}
		Also, we have
		\begin{align}\label{Kaiss}
			\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(z) - \Gamma_{z,w}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w)
			&=
			\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(z) - \Gamma_{z,\tilde{z}}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(\tilde{z})
			+ \Gamma_{z,\tilde{z}}
			\bigl(
			\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(\tilde{z}) - \Gamma_{\tilde{z},w}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w)
			\bigr).
		\end{align}
		Using \eqref{BB1} and \eqref{BB2}, we obtain
		\begin{align}\label{Oalss}
			\begin{split}
				&\frac{\Vert {z}\Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1}\big\Vert \Gamma_{z,\tilde{z}}
					\bigl(
					\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(\tilde{z}) - \Gamma_{\tilde{z},w}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w)
					\bigr)\big\Vert_a}{d(z,w)^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}} \\
				&\leq \|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+T}^s}}
				\sum_{\substack{a_1\in\mathcal{A}^+,\\ |a|_h\leq|a_1|_h}}
				\frac{d(z,\tilde{z})^{|a_1|_h-|a|_h}d(\tilde{z},w)^{\gamma_1-|a_1|_h}}{d(z,w)^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}}
				\frac{\Vert {z}\Vert_{P_s}^{\gamma_1}}{\Vert \tilde{z}\Vert_{P_s}^{\gamma_1}}
				\frac{
					\Vert \tilde{z}\Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1}
					\, \Vert \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(\tilde{z}) - \Gamma_{\tilde{z},w}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w) \Vert_{a_1}
				}{
					d(\tilde{z},w)^{\gamma_1-|a_1|_h}
				}.
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		Therefore, from \eqref{cond:A} and \eqref{2369}--\eqref{Oalss}, for every
		$a \in \mathcal{A}^+$ we have
		\begin{align}\label{BNasa}
			\begin{split}
				&\frac{
					\Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1}
					\, \Vert \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(z) - \Gamma_{z,w}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w) \Vert_{a}
				}{
					d(z,w)^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}
				}
				\lesssim
				\frac{
					\Vert z\Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1}
					\, \Vert \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(z) - \Gamma_{z,\tilde{z}}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(\tilde{z}) \Vert_{a}
				}{
					d(z,\tilde{z})^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}
				} \\
				&\quad +
				\|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+T}^s}}
				\sum_{\substack{a_1\in\mathcal{A}^+,\\ |a|_h\leq|a_1|_h}}
				\frac{
					\Vert \tilde{z}\Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1}
					\, \Vert \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(\tilde{z}) - \Gamma_{\tilde{z},w}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w) \Vert_{a_1}
				}{
					d(\tilde{z},w)^{\gamma_1-|a_1|_h}
				}.
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		Consequently, by assuming \eqref{cond:A}, in order to estimate \eqref{Kmasas},
		we need to estimate the following two terms separately:
		\begin{itemize}
			\item
			\begin{align}\label{NMass}
				\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}^+}
				\frac{
					\Vert z\Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1}
					\, \Vert \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(z) - \Gamma_{z,\tilde{z}}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(\tilde{z}) \Vert_{a}
				}{
					d(z,\tilde{z})^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}
				}
			\end{align}
			\item
			\begin{align}\label{NMas1s}
				\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}^+}\frac{
					\Vert \tilde{z}\Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1}
					\, \Vert \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(\tilde{z}) - \Gamma_{\tilde{z},w}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w) \Vert_{a}
				}{
					d(\tilde{z},w)^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}
				}
			\end{align}
		\end{itemize}
		The main task is to estimate \eqref{NMass}, which is the focus of the subsequent step.
		
		\textbf{Step 2.}\label{Step2}
		To estimate \eqref{NMass}, we first define
		\begin{align}\label{UJnasexs}
			(r_{-1}, r_0, r_1)=
			\begin{cases}
				(s, t_0, s+\delta_0) & \text{if } 0 < t_0 - s \leq\frac{\delta_0}{2}, \\
				\left(t_{0}-\frac{\delta_0}{2}, t_0, t_0+\frac{\delta_0}{2}\right) & \text{otherwise.}
			\end{cases}
		\end{align}
		
		With this definition, we set
		\begin{align*}
			k := \min \left\{ j \geq 0 : r_0 < t_{1} - \frac{j \delta_0}{2} \leq r_1 \right\}.
		\end{align*}
		Note that, since $r_{1}-r_{0}\geq \frac{\delta_0}{2}$, such a value exists.
		\begin{enumerate}[label=\textbf{(\Roman*)}]
			\item\label{I1} If $k=0$, we have
			\[
			0<t_1-t_0\leq \frac{\delta_0}{2}.
			\]
			We update $r_1=t_1$. Thus,
			\begin{align*}
				(r_{-1}, r_0, r_1)=
				\begin{cases}
					(s, t_0, t_1) & \text{if } 0 < t_0 - s \leq \frac{\delta_0}{2}, \\
					\left(t_{0} - \frac{\delta_0}{2}, t_0, t_1\right) & \text{otherwise.}
				\end{cases}
			\end{align*}
			We set $w_0 = z$ and $w_1 = \tilde{z}$. Then, together with \eqref{cond:A}
			\begin{align*}
				w_0, w_1 &\in O_{r_1}^{r_{-1}}, \quad r_1 - r_{-1} \leq \delta_0, \\
				d(w_0,w_1)=\sqrt{t_1-t_0}
				&\leq \| w_0,w_1 \|_{P_{r_{-1}}}= 
				\begin{cases}
					\| w_0 \|_{P_s} & \text{if } 0 < t_0 - s \leq \frac{\delta_0}{2}, \\
					\sqrt{\frac{\delta_0}{2}} & \text{otherwise.}
				\end{cases}
			\end{align*}
			         
			\item \label{I2} If $k=1$, we set $r_2 = t_1$ and
			\begin{align*}
				w_{0} = z = (t_0, y_0), \quad 
				w_{1} = (r_1, y_0), \quad 
				w_{2} = \tilde{z} = (r_2, y_0).
			\end{align*}
			Since $r_{1} < t_1 \leq r_1 + \frac{\delta_0}{2}$, we conclude that
			\begin{align*}
				w_0, w_1 &\in O^{r_{-1}}_{r_1}, \quad
				r_{1} - r_{-1} = \delta_0, \\
				d(w_0,w_1)
				&
				\leq \| w_0 \|_{P_{r_{-1}}}
				=\sqrt{\frac{\delta_0}{2}} 
			\end{align*}
			Note that, from \eqref{2369} and \eqref{cond:A}, one can easily see that for $r_1$ in \eqref{UJnasexs}, we must have
			\begin{align*}
				r_1=t_0+\frac{\delta_0}{2}.
			\end{align*}
			Otherwise, this contradicts $r_1<t_1\leq r_1+\frac{\delta_0}{2}.$
			Thus,
			\begin{align*}
				& w_1, w_2 \in O_{r_2}^{r_1 - \frac{\delta_0}{2}}, \quad
				r_2 - \left(r_1 - \frac{\delta_0}{2}\right) \leq \delta_0, \\
				& d(w_1,w_2)\leq \| w_1,w_2 \|_{P_{r_1 - \frac{\delta_0}{2}}}
				= \sqrt{\frac{\delta_0}{2}}.
			\end{align*}
			Also,
			\begin{align}\label{Bnassa}
				\max\big\{ d(w_0,w_1),\, d(w_1,w_{2}) \big\} \leq d(w_0,w_2).
			\end{align}
			\item \label{I3}  If $k > 1$, we set
			\begin{align*}
				r_j &= r_1 + \frac{(j-1)\delta_0}{2}, \quad 1 < j \leq k, & 
				w_j &= (r_j, y_0), \quad 0 \leq j \leq k, \\
				r_{k+1} &= t_1, &
				w_{k+1} &= (t_1, y_0).
			\end{align*}
			In particular, we have
			\begin{align}\label{Nnasss}
				\max\big\{ d(w_0,w_j),\, d(w_j,w_{j+1}) \big\} \leq d(w_0,w_{k+1}), \quad 0 \leq j \leq k.
			\end{align}
			Moreover, we must have $t_{0}-s>\frac{\delta_0}{2}$  and $	r_1=t_0+\frac{\delta_0}{2}.$ Also
			\begin{align}\label{UJmss}
				w_0,w_1
				&\in O^{r_{-1}}_{r_1}, \quad
				r_1-r_{-1}=\delta_0, \quad
				d(w_0,w_1)
				\leq
				\|w_0,w_1\|_{P_{r_{-1}}}
				=
				\sqrt{\frac{\delta_0}{2}}
				\notag\\
				w_j,w_{j+1}
				&\in O^{r_{j-1}}_{r_{j+1}}, \quad
				r_{j+1}-r_{j-1}=\delta_0, \quad
				d(w_j,w_{j+1})
				\leq
				\|w_j,w_{j+1}\|_{P_{r_{j-1}}}
				=
				\sqrt{\frac{\delta_0}{2}},
				\quad 1\leq j<k,
				\notag\\
				w_k,w_{k+1}
				&\in O^{r_{k-1}}_{r_{k+1}}, \quad
				r_{k+1}-r_{k-1}\leq\delta_0, \quad
				d(w_k,w_{k+1})
				\leq
				\|w_k,w_{k+1}\|_{P_{r_{k-1}}}
				=
				\sqrt{\frac{\delta_0}{2}}.
			\end{align}
			\begin{center}
				\begin{tikzpicture}[scale=.7]
					
					\definecolor{mygreen}{RGB}{50,180,50}
					
					                
					\draw[thick] (-1,0) rectangle (18,5);
					
					                          
					\draw[dashed] (1,0) -- (1,5);
					\draw[dashed] (2.2,0) -- (2.2,5);
					\draw[dashed] (3.5,0) -- (3.5,5);
					\draw[dashed] (5,0) -- (5,5);
					\draw[dashed] (8,0) -- (8,5);
					\draw[dashed] (9.5,0) -- (9.5,5);
					\draw[dashed] (11,0) -- (11,5);
					\draw[dashed] (15,0) -- (15,5);
					\draw[dashed] (18,0) -- (18,5);
					
					        
					\fill[mygreen] (2.2,2.5) circle (2pt);
					\node[right] at (2.2,2.7) {$w_0$};
					
					\fill[mygreen] (3.5,2.5) circle (2pt);
					\node[right] at (3.5,2.7) {$w_1$};
					
					\fill[mygreen] (5,2.5) circle (2pt);
					\node[right] at (5,2.7) {$w_2$};
					
					\node[right] at (6,2) {$\cdots$};
					
					\fill[mygreen] (8,2.5) circle (2pt);
					\node[left] at (8.2,2.7) {$w_{j-1}$};
					
					\fill[mygreen] (9.5,2.5) circle (2pt);
					\node[right] at (9.5,2.7) {$w_j$};
					
					\fill[mygreen] (11,2.5) circle (2pt);
					\node[right] at (11,2.7) {$w_{j+1}$};
					
					\node[right] at (12.5,2) {$\cdots$};
					
					\fill[mygreen] (15,2.5) circle (2pt);
					\node[right] at (15,2.7) {$w_{k+1}$};
					
					               
					\node at (1,-0.4) {$r_{-1}$};
					\node at (2.2,-0.4) {$r_0$};
					\node at (3.5,-0.4) {$r_1$};
					\node at (5,-0.4) {$r_2$};
					\node at (8,-0.4) {$r_{j-1}$};
					\node at (9.5,-0.4) {$r_j$};
					\node at (11,-0.4) {$r_{j+1}$};
					\node at (15,-0.4) {$r_{k+1}$};
					
					            
					\node[red] at (-1,-0.4) {$s$};
					\node[red] at (18,-0.4) {$s+T$};
					
					                         
					\draw[black,dash dot] (-1,2.5) -- (18,2.5);
					
					               
					\node[left] at (-1.2,2.8) {\textcolor{red}{$\mathbb{T}^d$}};
					
					                 
					\draw[blue,thick] (1,0) rectangle (3.5,5);
					\draw[red,dash dot,very thick] (2.2,0) rectangle (5,5);
					\draw[brown,thick] (8,0) rectangle (11,5);
					
				\end{tikzpicture}
			\end{center}
		\end{enumerate}
		We now continue the analysis for \eqref{I3}, i.e., the case $k > 1$.
		The remaining two cases, \eqref{I1} and \eqref{I2}, are simpler and can be handled analogously.
		First, note that, by \eqref{ASASa} and \eqref{UJmss},
		\begin{align}\label{NMAsse}
			\max\left\{
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_{r_{-1}} \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{r_1}^{r_{-1}}}^{(r_{-1})},  \,
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_{r_{j-1}} \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{r_{j+1}}^{r_{j-1}}}^{(r_{j-1})}
			: 1 \leq j \leq k
			\right\}
			\leq \Lambda.
		\end{align}
		Also, we know that
		\[
		\Gamma_{w_j,w_{j+1}} \circ \Gamma_{w_{j+1},w_{k+1}} = \Gamma_{w_j,w_{k+1}}, \quad \text{for } 0 \leq j \leq k-1.
		\]
		Thus,
		\begin{align}\label{Bnasz}
			\begin{split}
				&\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(z)
				-
				\Gamma_{z,\tilde{z}}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(\tilde{z})
				=
				\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_0)
				-
				\Gamma_{w_0,w_{k+1}}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_{k+1})
				\\
				&=
				\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_0)
				-
				\Gamma_{w_0,w_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_1)
				+
				\sum_{1\leq j\leq k}
				\Gamma_{w_0,w_j}
				\Big(
				\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_j)
				-
				\Gamma_{w_j,w_{j+1}}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_{j+1})
				\Big).
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		Let us define
		\begin{align*}
			D(w_0,w_j,a,a_1)
			&= d(w_{0},w_{j})^{|a_1|_h-|a|_h}\, d(w_{j},w_{j+1})^{\gamma_1-|a_1|_{h}} \\
			&\quad \text{for } a,a_1 \in \mathcal{A}^{+} = \{ \large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}},  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
, \large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}} : i=1,\ldots,d \}, \quad |a|_h \leq |a_1|_h, \qquad 1 \leq j \leq k.
		\end{align*}
		Then, by \eqref{BB1}, \eqref{BB2}, and \eqref{Bnasz}, for each $a \in \mathcal{A}^{+}$, we obtain
		\begin{align}\label{Bnass}
			\begin{split}
				&\Vert \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} (z) - \Gamma_{z,\tilde{z}} \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} (\tilde{z}) \Vert_a\leq\Vert \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_0) - \Gamma_{w_0,w_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_1)\Vert_{a}
				+ \sum_{1\leq j\leq k} \big\Vert \Gamma_{w_0,w_j} \big(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_j) - \Gamma_{w_j,w_{j+1}}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_{j+1}) \big) \big\Vert_{a}\\& \lesssim d(w_0,w_1)^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}
				\frac{\Vert \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_0) - \Gamma_{w_0,w_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_1)\Vert_{a}}
				{d(w_0,w_1)^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}} \\
				                                      
				                                                           
				                             
				                                                                                        
				&	+ \|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+T}^s}}
				\sum_{1\leq j\leq k}\sum_{\substack{a_1\in\mathcal{A}^+,\\ |a|_h\leq|a_1|_h}}
				d(w_0,w_j)^{|a_1|_h-|a|_h}
				\Vert \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_j) - \Gamma_{w_j,w_{j+1}}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_{j+1}) \Vert_{a_1} \\
				&= \frac{d(w_0,w_1)^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}}{\| w_0,w_1 \|_{P_{r_{-1}}}^{\gamma_1}}
				\frac{	\| w_0,w_1 \|_{P_{r_{-1}}}^{\gamma_1}\Vert \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_0) - \Gamma_{w_0,w_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_1)\Vert_{a}}
				{d(w_0,w_1)^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}}\\
				& + \|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+T}^s}}
				\sum_{1\leq j\leq k}\sum_{\substack{a_1\in\mathcal{A}^+,\\ |a|_h\leq|a_1|_h}}
				\frac{
					D(w_0,w_j,a,a_1)
				}{
					\Vert w_j,w_{j+1} \Vert_{P_{r_{j-1}}}^{\gamma_1}
				}
				\frac{
					\Vert w_j,w_{j+1} \Vert_{P_{r_{j-1}}}^{\gamma_1}
					\Vert \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_j) - \Gamma_{w_j,w_{j+1}}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_{j+1}) \Vert_{a_1}
				}{
					d(w_j,w_{j+1})^{\gamma_1-|a_1|_h}
				}.
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		Thus, it follows from \eqref{Nnasss}, \eqref{UJmss} and \eqref{Bnass} that
		\begin{align}\label{HNasda}
			\begin{split}
				&\frac{\Vert w_0,w_{k+1}\Vert_{P_s}^{\gamma_1}\Vert\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} (w_0) - \Gamma_{w_0,w_{k+1}} \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} (w_{k+1})\Vert_{a}}{d(w_0,w_{k+1})^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}}\leq\frac{\Vert w_0,w_{k+1}\Vert_{P_s}^{\gamma_1}\Vert\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_0) - \Gamma_{w_0,w_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_1)\Vert_{a}}{d(w_0,w_{k+1})^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}}\\&+\sum_{1\leq j\leq k} \frac{\Vert w_0,w_{k+1}\Vert_{P_s}^{\gamma_1}\big\Vert\Gamma_{w_0,w_j} \big(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_j) - \Gamma_{w_j,w_{j+1}}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_{j+1}) \big)\big\Vert_{a} }{d(w_0,w_{k+1})^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}} \\&\lesssim \frac{\Vert w_0,w_{k+1}\Vert_{P_s}^{\gamma_1}}{\| w_0,w_1 \|_{P_{r_{-1}}}^{\gamma_1}}\frac{d(w_0,w_1)^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}}{d(w_0,w_{k+1})^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}}\frac{\| w_0,w_1 \|_{P_{r_{-1}}}^{\gamma_1}\Vert\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_0) - \Gamma_{w_0,w_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_1)\Vert_{a}}{d(w_0,w_1)^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}}                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               
				\\&+ \|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+T}^s}}	\sum_{1\leq j \leq k}\sum_{\substack{a_1\in\mathcal{A}^+,\\ |a|_h\leq|a_1|_h}}\frac{\Vert w_0,w_{k+1}\Vert_{P_s}^{\gamma_1}}{\Vert w_{j},w_{j+1}\Vert_{P_{r_{j-1}}}^{\gamma_1}}\frac{D(w_0,w_j,a,a_1)}{d(w_0,w_{k+1})^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}}\frac{\Vert w_{j},w_{j+1}\Vert_{P_{r_{j-1}}}^{\gamma_1}\Vert\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_j) - \Gamma_{w_j,w_{j+1}}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w_{j+1}) \Vert_{a_1}}{d(w_{j},w_{j+1})^{\gamma_1-|a_1|_{h}}}\\&\lesssim \left(\frac{1}{\delta_0}\right)^{\frac{\gamma_1+2}{2}} \|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+T}^s}}\Lambda.
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		Note that, to derive the above bound, we also used \eqref{NMAsse} and the fact that $k\lesssim \frac{1}{\delta_0}$. It is easy to see that this bound also holds in the two cases \eqref{I1} and \eqref{I2}.
		To summarize, from \eqref{HNasda}, we conclude that, by assuming \eqref{cond:A},
		\begin{align}\label{NBN}
			\sup_{\substack{a\in\mathcal{A}^+}}
			\frac{\Vert z,\tilde{z} \Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1} \, \Vert \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(z) - \Gamma_{z,\tilde{z}} \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(\tilde{z}) \Vert_{a}}{d(z,\tilde{z})^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}}
			\lesssim \left(\frac{1}{\delta_0}\right)^{\frac{\gamma_1+2}{2}} \|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+T}^s}}\Lambda.
		\end{align}
		
		\textbf{Step 3.}\label{Step3}
		Now we estimate \eqref{NMas1s}. This is quite straightforward.
		First, recall that $\tilde{z}=(t_1,y_0)$ and $w=(t_1,y_1)$.
		There are two possible cases:
		\begin{itemize}
			\item
			If $t_1-s\leq\delta_0$, then $\tilde{z},w\in O_{t_1}^{s}$ and,
			thanks to \eqref{ASASa} and \eqref{2369},
			\begin{align*}
				\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}^+}
				\frac{
					\Vert \tilde{z}\Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1}
					\, \Vert \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(\tilde{z})
					- \Gamma_{\tilde{z},w}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w) \Vert_{a}
				}{
					d(\tilde{z},w)^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}
				}
				\leq \Lambda.
			\end{align*}
			
			\item If $t_1-s>\delta_0$, then by considering the straight line
			between $y_0$ and $y_1$, we define a sequence
			$(\bar{y}_{j})_{0\leq j\leq m}$ such that
			\begin{align*}
				&\bar{y}_{0}=y_0,\qquad
				\bar{y}_{m}=y_1,\qquad
				\Vert \bar{y}_{m}-\bar{y}_{m-1}\Vert_{\mathbb{T}^d}
				\leq \delta_0,
				\\
				&\Vert \bar{y}_{j}-\bar{y}_{j-1}\Vert_{\mathbb{T}^d}
				=\delta_0,
				\qquad 1\leq j<m.
			\end{align*}
			In this way, by setting $\bar{w}_{j}=(t_1,\bar{y}_{j})$, we have
			\begin{align*}
				\bar{w}_j,\bar{w}_{j+1}
				&\in O^{t_1-\delta_0}_{t_1},
				\\
				d(\bar{w}_j,\bar{w}_{j+1})
				&\leq
				\|\bar{w}_j,\bar{w}_{j+1}\|_{P_{t_1-\delta_0}}
				=
				\sqrt{\delta_0},
				\\
				&\hspace{3.8cm}
				0\leq j<m,
				\qquad
				m\lesssim\frac{1}{\delta_0}.
			\end{align*}
			
			Similarly to \eqref{Bnasz},
			\begin{align}\label{Mkopass}
				\begin{split}
					&\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(\tilde{z})
					-\Gamma_{\tilde{z},w}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w)
					\\
					&=
					\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(\bar{w}_0)
					-\Gamma_{\bar{w}_0,\bar{w}_1}
					\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(\bar{w}_1)+
					\sum_{1\leq j<m}
					\Gamma_{\bar{w}_0,\bar{w}_j}
					\Big(
					\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(\bar{w}_j)
					-\Gamma_{\bar{w}_j,\bar{w}_{j+1}}
					\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(\bar{w}_{j+1})
					\Big).
				\end{split}
			\end{align}
			Using the same argument as in \eqref{Bnass} and \eqref{HNasda},
			we obtain from \eqref{Mkopass} that
			\begin{align}\label{NNMa}
				\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}^+}
				\frac{
					\Vert\tilde{z},w\Vert_{P_s}^{\gamma_1}
					\,\Vert
					\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(\tilde{z})
					-\Gamma_{\tilde{z},w}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w)
					\Vert_a
				}{
					d(\tilde{z},w)^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}
				}
				\lesssim
				\left(\frac{1}{\delta_0}\right)^{\frac{\gamma_1+2}{2}}
				\|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+T}^s}}
				\Lambda.
			\end{align}
		\end{itemize}
		\textbf{Step 4.}\label{Step4} Now we finalize the proof. From \eqref{BNasa}, \eqref{NBN}, and \eqref{NNMa}, we conclude that, by assuming \eqref{cond:A},
		\begin{align*}
			\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}^+}\sup_{\substack{ z,w\in O_{T+s}^s,\ z\neq w\\ d(z,w)\leq\|z,w\|_{P_s}\\ \|z,w\|_{P_s}=\|z\|_{P_s} }}
			\frac{
				\Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1}
				\, \Vert \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(z) - \Gamma_{z,w}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w) \Vert_{a}
			}{
				d(z,w)^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}
			}
			\lesssim \left(\frac{1}{\delta_0}\right)^{\frac{\gamma_1+2}{2}}
			\big( \|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+T}^s}}\big)^2 \Lambda.
		\end{align*}
		As mentioned earlier, the assumption \eqref{cond:A} was made for simplicity, and the above argument from the beginning until here can be adapted to the case $z,w \in O_{T+s}^s$ with $\|z,w\|_{P_s} = \|w\|_{P_s}$. Thus, to summarize, we can state that
		\begin{align}\label{Bczxxxxz}
			\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}^+}\sup_{\substack{ z,w\in O_{T+s}^s,\ z\neq w\\ d(z,w)\leq\|z,w\|_{P_s} }}
			\frac{
				\Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1}
				\, \Vert \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(z) - \Gamma_{z,w}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w) \Vert_{a}
			}{
				d(z,w)^{\gamma_1-|a|_h}
			}
			\lesssim \left(\frac{1}{\delta_0}\right)^{\frac{\gamma_1+2}{2}}
			\big( \|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+T}^s}}\big)^2 \Lambda.
		\end{align}
		Also note that, from \eqref{ASASa}, for $z = (t_0,x)$ and $a\in \mathcal{A}^+$, we have
		\begin{align}\label{MBM}
			\Vert z\Vert_{P_s}^{|a|_{h}}\Vert\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} (z)\Vert_{a}
			\leq 
			\begin{cases}
				\Lambda, & \text{if } \sqrt{t_0 - s} = \Vert z\Vert_{P_s} \leq \sqrt{\delta_0}, \\[0.5em]
				\displaystyle
				\frac{\Vert z\Vert_{P_s}^{|a|_{h}}}{\Vert z\Vert_{P_{t_0-\delta_0}}^{|a|_{h}}}
				\Vert z\Vert_{P_{t_0-\delta_0}}^{|a|_{h}}
				\Vert\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(z)\Vert_{a}
				\lesssim \left(\frac{1}{\delta_0}\right)^{\frac{\gamma_1}{2}} \Lambda,
				& \text{otherwise}.
			\end{cases}
		\end{align}
		
		The claim then follows from \eqref{Bczxxxxz} and \eqref{MBM}.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{Alex}
		Let $s\in\mathbb{R}$ and $T\in(0,1]$. Suppose that
		$\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}$ is a solution to \eqref{MAIN_1} on $O^s_{s+T}$. Then
		\begin{align}\label{SSAS78sasassss}
			\begin{split}
				&\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{s+T}^s}^{(s)}
				\lesssim P_{1}\Big(\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{s+1+T}^{s-1}}},[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
\ ]_{\overline{{O}_{T+s}^s}}, \|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+T}^s}},\sup_{w\in O_{s+T}^s}
				\|\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)\|,\|u_0\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}\Big)	
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		where
		\begin{align}\label{ASasas78qw}
			\begin{split}
				P_{1}(b,c,d,f,g):=&
				d^2
				(1+b^2)^{\frac{\gamma_1+2}{1-\kappa}}
				\big(1+|\mu|+c+f^{\gamma_1-1}\big)^{\frac{\gamma_1+2}{1-\kappa}}
				\big(
				f+g+(1+b^2)(1+c^2+f^{\gamma_1})
				\big),
				\\
				&\qquad \text{for } b,c,d,f,g \geq 0.
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
The proof is a bit lengthy, so we defer it to Appendix~\ref{Alexi}.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{ATGFRAs}
		Assume that   $n \ge 3$ and $\Sigma \in C^{n}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R})$. Then, for every $T>0$, the map
		\begin{align}\label{TGBG}
			\begin{split}
				\overline{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_T &: 
				L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}) \times 
				\mathcal{D}^{\gamma_1,0}_{P_{s},0}\big|_{O_{T+s}} 
				\longrightarrow 
				\mathcal{D}^{\gamma_1,0}_{P_{s},0}\big|_{O_{T+s}}, \\
				\overline{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_T(\tilde{u},\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}) &=
				\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{0},1-\kappa}
				\Big(
				\mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\Sigma(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} + \textcolor{red}{G^{s}}\tilde{u}) \,\tilde{\star}\, \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
				\Big)+\mu\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{1},2}\big(\mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} + \textcolor{red}{G^{s}}(\tilde{u})\big)
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		is $C^{\,n-2}$–Fréchet differentiable in both of its arguments.
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
For $i=1,2$, set
		\[
		\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}}_i
		:=
		\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_i+\textcolor{red}{G^s}\tilde{u}_i.
		\]
		For \(1 \leq j \leq n-2\), the composition estimates \eqref{TYUa1}, together with \eqref{multi}, yield
		\begin{align}\label{TYLOR}
			\begin{split}
				\Sigma(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}_2}) - \Sigma(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}_1})
				&=
				\sum_{i=1}^{j}\frac{1}{i!}
				\Sigma^{(i)}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}_1})
				\star^{i}
				\bigl(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}_2}-\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}_1}\bigr)+o\!\left(
				\left(
				\lvert\!\lVert \mathcal{V}_2-\mathcal{V}_1 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,\,0,\,O_{T+s}}^{(s)}
				\right)^{j}
				\right).
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
Here the little-$o$ notation has its usual meaning.
		Since $\textcolor{red}{G^s}$ is linear and continuous,
		\[
		\lvert\!\lVert \mathcal{V}_2-\mathcal{V}_1 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,\,0,\,O_{T+s}}^{(s)}
		\lesssim
		\lvert\!\lVert \mathcal{U}_2-\mathcal{U}_1 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,\,0,\,O_{T+s}}^{(s)}
		+
		\|\tilde{u}_2-\tilde{u}_1\|_{L^\infty(\mathbb{T}^d)}.
		\]
		Since $\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{0},1-\kappa}$,
		$\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{1},2}$,
		and $\textcolor{red}{G^{s}}$ are linear and continuous, the desired conclusion follows from \eqref{TYLOR}.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{YHATSs}
		Let $n \geq 3$ and $\Sigma \in C^{n}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R})$. Assume that, for a given initial condition
		$u_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})$, there exist constants $T>0$ and $\sigma>0$, and a map
		\[
		\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T} :
		B_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}(u_{0},\sigma)
		\longrightarrow
		\mathcal{D}^{\gamma_1,0}_{P_s,0}\big|_{O_{T+s}},
		\]
		such that, for every
		$\tilde{u}\in B_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}(u_{0},\sigma)$,
		the modelled distribution
		$\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(\tilde{u})=\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{\tilde{u}}$
		is the solution of \eqref{MAIN_1} on $[s,s+T]$ with initial condition
		$\tilde{u}$. Then
		\[
		\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}
		\in
		C^{\,n-2}\!\left(
		B_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}(u_{0},\sigma),
		\,
		\mathcal{D}^{\gamma_1,0}_{P_s,0}\big|_{O_{T+s}}
		\right).
		\]
		Moreover, for each fixed $t\in[0,T]$, define
		\[
		\mathcal{J}_{t} :
		B_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}(u_{0},\sigma)
		\longrightarrow
		L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),
		\qquad
		\mathcal{J}_{t}(\tilde{u})
		:=
		\mathcal{R}\!\left(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(\tilde{u})\right)(s+t,\cdot).
		\]
		Then
		\[
		\mathcal{J}_{t}
		\in
		C^{\,n-2}\!\left(
		B_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}(u_{0},\sigma),
		L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
		\right),
		\]
		and
		\[
		\mathcal{J}_{0}=\mathrm{Id}.
		\]
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
First, by Lemma~\ref{ATGFRAs}, the map
		$\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{T}^{s}$ is $C^{\,n-2}$--Fréchet differentiable.
		Moreover, using \eqref{FIXED}, \eqref{fixed-point}, and Lemma~\ref{stat},
		we can choose $T_0\in(0,T]$ and $\sigma_0\in(0,\sigma]$ sufficiently small
		such that, for some $M>0$,
		\[
		\overline{\mathcal Q}_{T_0}^{s}:
		\overline{B_{L^\infty(\mathbb T^d)}(u_0,\sigma_0)}
		\times
		\overline{
			B_{\mathcal D^{\gamma_1,0}_{P_s,0}|_{O_{T_0+s}}}(0,M)}
		\longrightarrow
		\overline{
			B_{\mathcal D^{\gamma_1,0}_{P_s,0}|_{O_{T_0+s}}}(0,M)}
		\]
		and
		\begin{align*}
			&\lvert\!\lVert 
				\overline{\mathcal Q}_{T_0}^{s}(u_2,\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}_2)
				-
				\overline{\mathcal Q}_{T_0}^{s}(u_1,\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}_1) \rVert\!\rvert
			_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T_0+s}}^{(s)}
			\\
			&\qquad\leq
			\frac12
			\left(
			\lvert\!\lVert 
				\mathbf R_s^+\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}_2-
				\mathbf R_s^+\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}_1 \rVert\!\rvert
			_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T_0+s}}^{(s)}
			+
			\|u_2-u_1\|_{L^\infty(\mathbb T^d)}
			\right).
		\end{align*}
		Therefore, for every
		$\tilde u\in B_{L^\infty(\mathbb T^d)}(u_0,\sigma_0)$,
		the map
		\[
		\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}\mapsto
		\overline{\mathcal Q}_{T_0}^{s}
		(\tilde u,\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U})
		\]
		is a contraction. Hence, by the contraction mapping theorem,
		there exists a unique fixed point $\textcolor{red}{g}(\tilde u)$ satisfying
		\begin{align}\label{IAS78asaas}
			\overline{\mathcal Q}_{T_0}^{s}
			(\tilde u,\textcolor{red}{g}(\tilde u))
			=
			\textcolor{red}{g}(\tilde u).
		\end{align}
		The corresponding solution can be written as
		\[
		\textcolor{red}{\mathcal J}_{T_0}(\tilde u)
		=
		\textcolor{red}{g}(\tilde u)+\textcolor{red}{G}^s(\tilde u).
		\]
		We now prove the regularity of the fixed point with respect to the
		initial condition. Fix
		$u_1\in B_{L^\infty(\mathbb T^d)}(u_0,\sigma_0)$.
		Let $N_{u_1}$ and $W_{u_1}$ be neighborhoods of $u_1$ and
		$\textcolor{red}{g}(u_1)$, respectively, such that
		\[
		\mathbf D:
		N_{u_1}\times W_{u_1}
		\to
		\mathcal D^{\gamma_1,0}_{P_s,0}|_{O_{T_0+s}}
		\]
		given by
		\[
		\mathbf D(\tilde u,\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U})
		=
		\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}
		-
		\overline{\mathcal Q}_{T_0}^{s}
		(\tilde u,\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U})
		\]
		is well defined. By \eqref{IAS78asaas},
		\[
		\mathbf D(u_1,\textcolor{red}{g}(u_1))=0.
		\]
		For fixed $\tilde u$, define
		\[
		\mathbf D_{\tilde u}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U})
		=
		\mathbf D(\tilde u,\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}).
		\]
		Its derivative with respect to $\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}$ is
		\[
		\frac{\partial\mathbf D_{\tilde u}}
		{\partial\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}}
		(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U})
		=
		I-
		\frac{\partial\overline{\mathcal Q}_{T_0}^{s}}
		{\partial\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}}
		(\tilde u,\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}).
		\]
		Set
		\[
		A_{\tilde u,\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}}
		=
		\frac{\partial\overline{\mathcal Q}_{T_0}^{s}}
		{\partial\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}}
		(\tilde u,\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}).
		\]
		The contraction estimate implies
		\[
		\|A_{\tilde u,\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}}\|\leq \frac12.
		\]
		Hence $I-A_{\tilde u,\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}}$ is invertible and its inverse
		is given by the Neumann series
		\[
		\left(
		\frac{\partial\mathbf D_{\tilde u}}
		{\partial\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}}
		(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U})
		\right)^{-1}
		=
		\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}
		A_{\tilde u,\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}}^{\,k},
		\]
		where $A_{\tilde u,\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}}^{\,k}$ denotes the $k$-fold
		composition of the same bounded linear operator.
		Therefore,
		\[
		\frac{\partial\mathbf D_{\tilde u}}
		{\partial\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}}
		(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U})
		\]
		is an isomorphism. Applying the implicit function theorem in Banach spaces
		\cite[Theorem~2.5.7]{AMR88}, we conclude that
		\[
		\textcolor{red}{g}:
		N_{u_1}\longrightarrow
		\mathcal D^{\gamma_1,0}_{P_s,0}|_{O_{T_0+s}}
		\]
		is of class $C^{n-2}$. Since $u_1$ was arbitrary, and since
		$\textcolor{red}{G}^s$ is linear and continuous, it follows that
		\[
		\textcolor{red}{\mathcal J}_{T_0}
		\in
		C^{n-2}\!\left(
		B_{L^\infty(\mathbb T^d)}(u_0,\sigma_0),
		\mathcal D^{\gamma_1,0}_{P_s,0}|_{O_{T_0+s}}
		\right).
		\]
		To extend the result to $[s,s+T]$, we use the restarting property.
		At time $s+T_0$, the initial condition for the restarted equation is
		\[
		\mathcal J_{T_0}(\tilde u).
		\]
		Since $\mathcal J_{T_0}$ is of class $C^{n-2}$ with respect to
		$\tilde u$, the same argument applied to the restarted equation shows
		that the solution depends $C^{n-2}$--smoothly on $\tilde u$ on the next
		time interval. Iterating this argument and using that $[s,s+T]$ can be
		covered by finitely many such intervals, we obtain
		\[
		\textcolor{red}{\mathcal J}_{T}
		\in
		C^{n-2}\!\left(
		B_{L^\infty(\mathbb T^d)}(u_0,\sigma),
		\mathcal D^{\gamma_1,0}_{P_s,0}|_{O_{T+s}}
		\right).
		\]
		Finally, for every fixed $t\in(0,T]$, the reconstruction and evaluation
		map
		\[
		\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U}
		\longmapsto
		\mathcal R(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal U})(s+t,\cdot)
		\]
		is continuous into $L^\infty(\mathbb T^d)$. Hence
		\[
		\mathcal J_t
		\in
		C^{n-2}\!\left(
		B_{L^\infty(\mathbb T^d)}(u_0,\sigma),
		L^\infty(\mathbb T^d)
		\right).
		\]
		For $t=0$, the initial condition gives
		\[
		\mathcal J_0=\mathrm{Id}.
		\]
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{kkkl}
		Consider the setting of Lemma \ref{YHATSs} and let 
		$u_1 \in B_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}(u_0, \sigma)$. Then the Fréchet derivative
		\[
		D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T} : L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d) \longrightarrow \mathcal{D}^{\gamma_1,0}_{P_{s},0}\big|_{O_{T+s}}
		\]
		is well-defined. Moreover, for any $\tilde{u} \in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)$, the derivative $D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}[\tilde{u}]$ satisfies the linearized equation on $[s,T+s]$:
		\begin{align}\label{MAIN2}
			\begin{cases}
				(\partial_t - \Delta) D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}[\tilde{u}]
				= \mu D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}[\tilde{u}]+\big(\Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1)) \star D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}[\tilde{u}]\big) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}, & t>s, \\
				\mathcal{R}\left(D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}[\tilde{u}]\right)(s,\cdot) = \tilde{u}, & t=s.
			\end{cases}
		\end{align}
		Equivalently, in mild form on $[s,s+T]$, we have
		\begin{align}\label{UIO}
			\mathbf{R}^+_{s}D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}[\tilde{u}]
			&=
			\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{0},1-\kappa}
			\Big(
			\big(\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1)) \star D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}[\tilde{u}]\big) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
			\Big)+\mu\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{1},2}(\mathbf{R}^+_{s} D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}[\tilde{u}])+ \textcolor{red}{G^{s}}(\tilde{u}).
		\end{align}
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
Thanks to Lemma~\ref{YHATSs}, the derivative operator exists. In particular, for every 
		$u_1 \in B_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}(u_0, \sigma)$ and $\tilde{u} \in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)$,
		\begin{align}\label{epsilon}
			\mathbf{R}^+_{s}D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}[\tilde{u}]
			=
			\lim_{\epsilon \to 0}
			\frac{\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1+\epsilon\tilde{u})
				-
				\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1)}{\epsilon}.
		\end{align}
		For sufficiently small $\epsilon$, set
		\begin{align*}
			\begin{split}
				&\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{H}_1}(u_1,\tilde{u},\epsilon)
				:=
				\frac{
					\Sigma\big(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1+\epsilon\tilde{u})\big)
					-
					\Sigma\big(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1)\big)
				}{\epsilon}
				-
				\Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1))
				\star
				D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}[\tilde{u}],
				\\
				&\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{H}_2}(u_1,\tilde{u},\epsilon)
				:=
				\frac{\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1+\epsilon\tilde{u})
					-
					\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1)}{\epsilon}
				-
				D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}[\tilde{u}].
			\end{split}
		\end{align*}
		Subtracting the mild equations satisfied by
		$\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1+\epsilon\tilde{u})$
		and $\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1)$, and dividing by $\epsilon$, it is sufficient to show that
		\begin{align}\label{Q0}
			\begin{split}
				&\lim_{\epsilon \to 0}
				\big\Vert\big\vert
				\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{0},1-\kappa}
				\big(
				\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{H}_1}(u_1,\tilde{u},\epsilon)
				\tilde{\star}
				\textcolor{red}{\bullet}
				\big)
				\big\vert\big\Vert_{\gamma_1,\,0,\,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}
				=0,
				\\
				&\lim_{\epsilon \to 0}
				\big\Vert\big\vert
				\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{1},2}
				\big(
				\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{H}_2}(u_1,\tilde{u},\epsilon)
				\big)
				\big\vert\big\Vert_{\gamma_1,\,0,\,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}
				=0.
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		Without loss of generality, we may assume that $T\in (0,1]$ and then apply a
		patching argument to treat arbitrary $T$. Thanks to \eqref{A2222}, \eqref{A222233},
		and \eqref{multi}, it suffices to prove that
		\begin{align}\label{UJNMa}
			\begin{split}
				&\lim_{\epsilon \to 0}
				\lvert\!\lVert 
					\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{H}_1}(u_1,\tilde{u},\epsilon)
				 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}
				=0,
				\\
				&\lim_{\epsilon \to 0}
				\lvert\!\lVert 
					\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{H}_2}(u_1,\tilde{u},\epsilon)
				 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}
				=0.
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		The second one follows from \eqref{epsilon}. Let us address the first one.
		To this end, by the definition of $\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{H}_1}$ and the Taylor expansion
		\eqref{TYUa}, we write
		\begin{align*}
			&\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{H}_1}(u_1,\tilde{u},\epsilon)
			\\
			&=
			\underbrace{
				\int_{0}^{1}
				\bigg[
				\Sigma'\Big(
				\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1)
				+
				\alpha\big(
				\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1+\epsilon\tilde{u})
				-
				\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1)
				\big)
				\Big)
				-
				\Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1))
				\bigg]
				\star
				\bigg[
				\frac{
					\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1+\epsilon\tilde{u})
					-
					\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1)
				}{\epsilon}
				\bigg]
				\,\mathrm{d}\alpha
			}_{=: \textcolor{red}{I}(u_1,\tilde{u},\epsilon)}
			\\
			&\quad+
			\underbrace{
				\Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1))
				\star
				\bigg[
				\frac{
					\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1+\epsilon\tilde{u})
					-
					\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1)
				}{\epsilon}
				-
				D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}[\tilde{u}]
				\bigg]
			}_{=: \textcolor{red}{II}(u_1,\tilde{u},\epsilon)} .
		\end{align*}
		From \eqref{epsilon}, \eqref{TYUa}, and \eqref{multi}, we conclude that
		\begin{align*}
			\lim_{\epsilon \to 0}
			\lvert\!\lVert 
				\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{I}(u_1,\tilde{u},\epsilon)
			 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}
			=0,
			\qquad
			\lim_{\epsilon \to 0}
			\lvert\!\lVert 
				\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{II}(u_1,\tilde{u},\epsilon)
			 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}
			=0.
		\end{align*}
		This yields \eqref{UJNMa}, and hence, after passing to the limit in the
		difference quotient of the mild equations,
		\begin{align*}
			\mathbf{R}^+_{s}D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}[\tilde{u}]
			&=
			\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{0},1-\kappa}
			\Big(
			\mathbf{R}^+_{s}
			\Big(
			\Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}(u_1))
			\star
			D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}[\tilde{u}]
			\Big)
			\tilde{\star}\textcolor{red}{\bullet}
			\Big)+
			\mu\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{1},2}
			\Big(
			\mathbf{R}^+_{s}D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}[\tilde{u}]
			\Big)
			+
			\textcolor{red}{G^{s}}(\tilde{u}).
		\end{align*}
		This proves \eqref{UIO}. Finally, since $\mathcal{J}_0=\mathrm{Id}$ by
		Lemma~\ref{YHATSs}, differentiating at $u_1$ in the direction $\tilde{u}$ yields
		\[
		D_{u_1}\mathcal{J}_0[\tilde{u}]=\tilde{u},
		\]
		and therefore
		\[
		\mathcal{R}\left(
		D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_T}[\tilde{u}]
		\right)(s,\cdot)
		=
		\tilde{u}.
		\]
		Hence \eqref{MAIN2} follows.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{onwall}
		Let 
		$\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}, \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}} \in \mathcal{D}^{\gamma_1,0}_{P_s,0}\big|_{O_{1+s}}$, 
		$\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}} \in \mathcal{D}^{\gamma_0,-1-\kappa}_{P_s}\big|_{O_{1+s}}$, 
		and $u \in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)$. 
		Assume that on $[s, 1+s] \times \mathbb{R}^d$ the following identity holds:
		\begin{align}\label{OL<45}
			\begin{cases}
				\mathbf{R}^+_s \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}
				=\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{0},1-\kappa}
				\Big(
				\big(\mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}} \star \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}\big) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
				+\mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}}
				\Big)+\mu\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{1},2}\left(\mathbf{R}^+_s \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}\right) + \textcolor{red}{G^{s}} (u), & t > s, \\
				\mathcal{R}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\cdot) = {u}, & t = s.
			\end{cases}
		\end{align}
		Then for the constants $\widetilde{C}_1,\widetilde{C}_2\geq 1$, we have
		\begin{align}\label{Atyasww}
			\begin{split}
				&\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}
				\\
				&\leq
				\widetilde{C}_1
				\big(\|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+1}^s}}\big)^2
				\big(1+\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{s+2}^{s-1}}}^2\big)^{
					\frac{\gamma_1+2}{1-\kappa}}
				\big(
				1+
				\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}} \rVert\!\rvert_
				{\gamma_1,0,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}
				\big)^{\frac{\gamma_1+2}{1-\kappa}}	
				\\
				&\times	\left(
				\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}} \rVert\!\rvert_
				{\gamma_0,-1-\kappa,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}
				+
				\Vert u \Vert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				\right)
				\exp\Big(
				\widetilde{C}_2
				\big(1+\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{s+2}^{s-1}}}^2\big)^{
					\frac{2}{1-\kappa}}
				\big(
				1+
				\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}} \rVert\!\rvert_
				{\gamma_1,0,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}
				\big)^{\frac{2}{1-\kappa}}
				\Big).
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
For better tracking of the proof, we divide it into several steps.
		
		\textbf{Step 1.}\label{Step10}  For $r \in [s,1+s]$, set
		\begin{align}\label{P1a}
			\mathbf{R}^+_r\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}^{r}} 
			:= & \mathcal{G}^{r}_{\gamma_{0},1-\kappa}
			\Big(
			(\mathbf{R}^+_r\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}} \star \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
			+ \mathbf{R}^+_r\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}}
			\Big)+\mu\mathcal{G}^{r}_{\gamma_{1},2}\left(\mathbf{R}^+_r \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}\right)
			+ \textcolor{red}{G^{r}} \big( \mathcal{R}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} (r,.)\big).
		\end{align}
		First, we show that
		\begin{align}\label{JMssa}
			\mathbf{R}^+_r \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} = \mathbf{R}^+_r \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}^{r}} 
			\quad \text{on } (r,1+s] \times \mathbb{R}^d.
		\end{align}
		To this end, since
		\[
		\mathbf{R}^+_r\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}^{r}},\mathbf{R}^+_r\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}
		\in \mathcal{D}^{\gamma_1,0}_{P_r,0}\big|_{O_{1+s}^r},
		\]
		by \cite[Proposition 3.29]{Hai14} and \eqref{521}, it suffices to show that 
		\begin{align}\label{Jmma}
			\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{R}^+_r \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}) 
			= \mathcal{R}(\mathbf{R}^+_r \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}^{r}}) 
			\quad \text{on } (r,1+s] \times \mathbb{R}^d.
		\end{align}
		From \eqref{A2222}, \eqref{A222233} and \eqref{IKLa}, we have
		\begin{align}\label{HYAs}
			\mathcal{R} \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(r,.)
			=
			\Big(G * \mathcal{R}
			\big(
			(\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}} \star \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
			+ \mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}} \big)
			+\mu G * \mathcal{R}\big(\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}\big)
			\Big)(r,.) + G^{s}(u)(r,.).
		\end{align}
		Again, using \eqref{A2222}, \eqref{A222233} and \eqref{IKLa}, together with \eqref{P1a} and \eqref{HYAs}, for $(t,x)\in (r,1+s] \times \mathbb{R}^d$, we have 
		\begin{align*}
			&\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{R}^+_r \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}^{r}})(t,x)
			=
			\Big(
			G * \mathcal{R}\big(
			(\mathbf{R}^+_{r}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}} \star \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
			+ \mathbf{R}^+_{r}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}}
			\big)
			+\mu G * \mathcal{R}\big(\mathbf{R}^+_{r}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}\big)
			\Big)(t,x)
			+ G^{r}\big( \mathcal{R}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} (r,.)\big)(t,x)
			\\
			&=
			\Big(
			G * \mathcal{R}\big(
			(\mathbf{R}^+_{r}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}} \star \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
			+ \mathbf{R}^+_{r}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}}
			\big)
			+\mu G * \mathcal{R}\big(\mathbf{R}^+_{r}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}\big)
			\Big)(t,x)
			\\
			&\quad
			+ G^{r}\Bigg(
			\Big(
			G * \mathcal{R}\big(
			(\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}} \star \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
			+ \mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}}
			\big)
			+\mu G * \mathcal{R}\big(\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}\big)
			\Big)(r,\cdot)
			+ (G^{s}u)(r,\cdot)
			\Bigg)(t,x).
		\end{align*}
		Consequently, using \eqref{SEMIJ},
		\begin{align*}
			\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{R}^+_r \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}^{r}})(t,x)
			&=
			\Big(
			G * \mathcal{R}\big(
			(\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}} \star \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
			+ \mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}}
			\big)
			+\mu G * \mathcal{R}\big(\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}\big)
			\Big)(t,x)
			+(G^{s}u)(t,x)
			\\
			&=
			\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{R}^+_s \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})(t,x)
			=
			\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{R}^+_r \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})(t,x).
		\end{align*}
		Therefore, \eqref{JMssa} is proved.
		\\
		\textbf{Step 2.}\label{Step20}
		We now proceed to prove the claimed inequality. In particular, we aim to apply Lemma~\ref{global}. We first verify its conditions.
		From \eqref{multi}, \eqref{A2222}, \eqref{A222233}, \eqref{A-1}, and Lemma~\ref{stat}, there exists a constant $C \geq 1$ such that, for each $\delta \in (0,1]$ and $r\in[s,s+1-\delta]$,
		\begin{align}\label{D0}
			\begin{split}
				\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_r\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}^r} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{\delta+r}^r}^{(r)}
				&\leq
				C\delta^{\frac{1-\kappa}{2}}
				\left(
				\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{r+1}^{r-1}}}^2+1
				\right)
				\left(
				1+
				\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_r\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{\delta+r}^r}^{(r)}
				\right)
				\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_r\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}^r} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{\delta+r}^r}^{(r)}
				\\
				&\quad+
				C\delta^{\frac{1-\kappa}{2}}
				\left(
				\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{r+1}^{r-1}}}^2+1
				\right)
				\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_r\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_0,-1-\kappa,O_{\delta+r}^r}^{(r)}+
				C\Vert \mathcal{R}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(r,\cdot)\Vert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}.
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		For an arbitrary $\beta \in (0,1)$, we define
		\begin{align}\label{kappa}
			\tilde{\delta}_{\beta}
			=
			\Bigg[
			\frac{\beta}{
				C\big(\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{s+2}^{s-1}}}^2+1\big)
				\left(
				1+\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{s+1}^s}^{(s)}
				\right)}
			\Bigg]^{\frac{2}{1-\kappa}}.
		\end{align}
		We note that
		\begin{align*}
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_r\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{\tilde{\delta}_{\beta}+r}^r}^{(r)}
			&\leq
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)},
			\\
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_r\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_0,-1-\kappa,O_{\tilde{\delta}_{\beta}+r}^r}^{(r)}
			&\leq
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_0,-1-\kappa,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}
		\end{align*}
		for all
		\[
		r\in[s,s+1-\tilde{\delta}_{\beta}].
		\]
		Thus, from \eqref{kappa},
		\begin{align}\label{OMaslsa}
			\begin{split}
				&\sup_{r\in[s,s+1-\tilde{\delta}_{\beta}]}
				C\tilde{\delta}_{\beta}^{\frac{1-\kappa}{2}}
				\left(
				\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{r+1}^{r-1}}}^2+1
				\right)
				\left(
				1+
				\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_r\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{\tilde{\delta}_{\beta}+r}^r}^{(r)}
				\right)
				\leq \beta,
				\\
				&\sup_{r\in[s,s+1-\tilde{\delta}_{\beta}]}
				C\tilde{\delta}_{\beta}^{\frac{1-\kappa}{2}}
				\left(
				\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{r+1}^{r-1}}}^2+1
				\right)
				\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_r\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_0,-1-\kappa,O_{\tilde{\delta}_{\beta}+r}^r}^{(r)}\leq
				\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_0,-1-\kappa,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}.
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		We choose a partition $\{r_i\}_{0\leq i\leq m}$ of $[s,s+1]$ such that
		\begin{align*}
			&r_0=s,\qquad r_m=s+1,\\
			&r_1-r_0\leq \tilde{\delta}_{\beta},\qquad
			r_m-r_{m-1}\leq \tilde{\delta}_{\beta},\\
			&r_{i+1}-r_i=\tilde{\delta}_{\beta},
			\qquad 1\leq i\leq m-2.
		\end{align*}
		Clearly, the number of subintervals satisfies
		\begin{align}\label{MAMSs}
			\frac{1}{\tilde{\delta}_{\beta}}
			\leq m
			\leq
			2+\frac{1}{\tilde{\delta}_{\beta}}.
		\end{align}
		Then from \eqref{D0} and \eqref{OMaslsa}, we obtain
		\begin{align}\label{D00}
			\begin{split}
				\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{r_1}^s}^{(s)}
				&\leq
				\beta
				\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{r_1}^s}^{(s)}+
				\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_0,-1-\kappa,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}
				+
				C\Vert u\Vert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}.
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		Thanks to \eqref{521}, we have $\mathcal{R}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(r_1,\cdot)
		=
		\mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}
		\big(
		\mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}
		\big)(r_1,\cdot),$
		and therefore, by \eqref{D00},
		\begin{align}\label{IKmals}
			\begin{split}
				\Vert \mathcal{R}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} (r_1,\cdot) \Vert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				&\leq
				\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{r_1}^s}^{(s)}
				\\
				&\leq
				\frac{
					\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_0,-1-\kappa,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}
					+
					C\Vert u\Vert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				}{1-\beta}.
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		It follows from \hyperref[Step10]{\textbf{Step 1}} that, for each
		$1\leq i\leq m-1$,
		\begin{align}\label{JMa}
			\mathbf{R}^+_{r_i}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}
			&=
			\mathcal{G}^{r_i}_{\gamma_{0},1-\kappa}
			\Big(
			(\mathbf{R}^+_{r_i}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}}
			\star
			\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})
			\tilde{\star}\textcolor{red}{\bullet}
			+
			\mathbf{R}^+_{r_i}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}}
			\Big)
			+\mu\mathcal{G}^{r_i}_{\gamma_{1},2}
			\left(
			\mathbf{R}^+_{r_i}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}
			\right)
			+
			\textcolor{red}{G^{r_i}}
			\big(
			\mathcal{R}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(r_i,\cdot)
			\big).
		\end{align}
		Again from \eqref{D0} and \eqref{OMaslsa}, we obtain
		\begin{align*}
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_{r_i}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{r_{i+1}}^{r_i}}^{(r_i)}
			&\leq
			\beta
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_{r_i}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{r_{i+1}}^{r_i}}^{(r_i)}
			+
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_0,-1-\kappa,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}
			+
			C\Vert \mathcal{R}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(r_i,\cdot)\Vert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}.
		\end{align*}
		Thus, since
		\[
		\mathcal{R}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(r_{i+1},\cdot)
		=
		\mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}
		\big(
		\mathbf{R}^+_{r_i}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}
		\big)(r_{i+1},\cdot),
		\]
		it follows that
		\begin{align}\label{IK45}
			\begin{split}
				\Vert\mathcal{R}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(r_{i+1},\cdot)\Vert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				&\leq
				\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_{r_i}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{r_{i+1}}^{r_i}}^{(r_i)}
				\\
				&\leq
				\frac{
					\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_0,-1-\kappa,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}
					+
					C\Vert \mathcal{R}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(r_i,\cdot)\Vert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				}{1-\beta}.
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		To summarize, from \eqref{IKmals} and \eqref{IK45}, for
		\[
		A_i
		:=
		\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_{r_i}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{r_{i+1}}^{r_i}}^{(r_i)},
		\qquad
		L_0
		:=
		\frac{
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_0,-1-\kappa,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}
		}{1-\beta},
		\qquad
		L_1
		:=
		\frac{C}{1-\beta},
		\]
		we have
		\begin{align*}
			A_i
			&\leq
			L_0+L_1A_{i-1},
			\qquad
			1\leq i\leq m-1,
			\\
			A_0
			&\leq
			L_0+L_1\Vert u\Vert_{L^\infty(\mathbb{T}^d)}.
		\end{align*}
		Thus, from the discrete Gr\"onwall lemma, we obtain
		\begin{align}\label{MAxAi}
			\max_{0\leq i\leq m-1}
			\left\{
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_{r_i}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{r_{i+1}}^{r_i}}^{(r_i)}
			\right\}
			\leq
			\frac{L_1^m-1}{L_1-1}L_0
			+
			L_1^m
			\Vert u\Vert_{L^\infty(\mathbb{T}^d)}.
		\end{align}
		Therefore, for arbitrary $b,c\in[s,s+1]$ with $0<c-b\leq\tilde{\delta}_\beta$, applying
		\eqref{D0}, \eqref{OMaslsa}, \eqref{MAMSs}, and \eqref{MAxAi}, we obtain
		\begin{align}\label{Bnas}
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_b\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_c^b}^{(b)}
			\lesssim
			L_1^{\frac{1}{\tilde{\delta}_\beta}+3}
			\left(
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_0,-1-\kappa,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}
			+
			\Vert u\Vert_{L^\infty(\mathbb{T}^d)}
			\right).
		\end{align}
		Thanks to \eqref{Bnas}, we are now in a position to apply Lemma~\ref{global} with $\tilde{\delta}_{\beta}$ defined in \eqref{kappa}. In particular for $\beta=\frac{1}{2}$, we obtain
		\begin{align*}
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}
			\lesssim
			\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{\delta}_{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)^{\frac{\gamma_1+2}{2}}
			\big(\|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+1}^s}}\big)^2
			L_{1}^{\frac{1}{\tilde{\delta}_{\frac{1}{2}}}+3}
			\left(
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_0,-1-\kappa,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}
			+\Vert u \Vert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}
			\right).
		\end{align*}
		Then, using \eqref{kappa} and after some straightforward algebraic manipulations, we obtain \eqref{Atyasww}.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
The identity \eqref{OL<45} is, in fact, equivalent to saying that 
		$\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}$ satisfies the following equation on $[s,1+s]$:
		\begin{align}\label{MAIN222}
			\begin{cases}
				(\partial_t-\Delta)\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}
				=
				\big(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}}\star\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}\big)
				\tilde{\star}\textcolor{red}{\bullet}
				+\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}}
				+\mu\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}, & t>s,\\
				\mathcal{R}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\cdot)=u, & t=s.
			\end{cases}
		\end{align}
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
The previous proposition can potentially be modified using the greedy-point technique; see, e.g., \cite{CLL13,GVR25,BG26}. In the case where $\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}}=\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}$ and $\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}}=0$, for Gaussian noises satisfying the corresponding assumptions required for the greedy-point estimates, this technique can be used to prove that the solutions to the parabolic Anderson model have finite moments of all orders. This is typically achieved by applying Borell's inequality. Since this technique is well established, we do not pursue the details here. See also \cite{XDQT21} for related moment bounds obtained using Feynman--Kac functional representations.
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{UJU}
		Consider the setting of Proposition~\ref{kkkl} with $T=1$ and assume that
		$\Sigma\in C_b^3(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R})$.
		Then there exist $\widetilde{C}_3,\widetilde{C}_4\geq 1$ such that, for any
		$u_1\in B_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}(u_0,\sigma)$ and
		$\tilde{u}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)$, we have
		\begin{align*}
			&\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}] \rVert\!\rvert
			_{\gamma_1,\,0,\,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}
			\\
			&\qquad\leq
			\mathcal{E}_1\Big(
			\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{s+2}^{s-1}}},
			[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
\ ]_{\overline{O_{s+1}^s}},
			\|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+1}^s}},
			\sup_{w\in O_{s+1}^s}
			\|\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_1}(w)\|,
			\|u_1\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}
			\Big)
			\Vert \tilde{u} \Vert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)},
		\end{align*}
		where
		\begin{align*}
			\mathcal{E}_1(b,c,d,f,g)
			&:=
			\widetilde{C}_3 P_2(b,c,d,f,g)
			\exp\Big(
			\widetilde{C}_4 P_3(b,c,d,f,g)
			\Big),
		\end{align*}
		with
		\begin{align*}
			P_{1}(b,c,d,f,g)
			:={}&
			d^2
			(1+b^2)^{\frac{\gamma_1+2}{1-\kappa}}
			\big(1+|\mu|+c+f^{\gamma_1-1}\big)^{\frac{\gamma_1+2}{1-\kappa}}
			\\
			&\times
			\big(
			f+g+(1+b^2)(1+c^2+f^{\gamma_1})
			\big),
		\end{align*}
		and
		\begin{align*}
			P_2(b,c,d,f,g)
			&:=
			d^2
			(1+b^2)^{\frac{\gamma_1+2}{1-\kappa}}
			\bigg(
			1+c^2+\big(P_1(b,c,d,f,g)\big)^2
			\bigg)^{\frac{\gamma_1+2}{1-\kappa}},
			\\
			P_3(b,c,d,f,g)
			&:=
			(1+b^2)^{\frac{2}{1-\kappa}}
			\bigg(
			1+c^2+\big(P_1(b,c,d,f,g)\big)^2
			\bigg)^{\frac{2}{1-\kappa}},
			\\
			&\qquad \text{for } b,c,d,f,g \geq 0.
		\end{align*}
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
Thanks to Proposition~\ref{kkkl}, we have
		\begin{align*}
			\mathbf{R}^+_{s}D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}]
			&=
			\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{0},1-\kappa}
			\Big(
			\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\Big(
			\Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_1))
			\star
			D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}]
			\Big)
			\tilde{\star}\textcolor{red}{\bullet}
			\Big)
			+\mu\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{1},2}
			\big(
			\mathbf{R}^+_{s}D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}]
			\big)
			+\textcolor{red}{G^{s}}(\tilde{u}).
		\end{align*}
		Then from Proposition \ref{onwall}, for the constants $\widetilde{C}_1,\widetilde{C}_2\geq 1$, we have
		\begin{align}
			\begin{split}
				&\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}] \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}\\&\leq \widetilde{C}_1
				\big(\|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+1}^s}}\big)^2
				\big(1+\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{s+2}^{s-1}}}^2\big)^{
					\frac{\gamma_1+2}{1-\kappa}}\big(\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s \Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_1)) \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}+1\big)^{\frac{\gamma_1+2}{1-\kappa}}	 \Vert \tilde{u} \Vert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}\\
				&\qquad\times\exp\Big(\widetilde{C}_2 \big(1+\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{s+2}^{s-1}}}^2\big)^{\frac{2}{1-\kappa}}\big(\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s \Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_1)) \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}+1\big)^{\frac{2}{1-\kappa}}	\Big).
			\end{split}
		\end{align} 
		The proof now follows from Remark~\ref{BOUNDED} and Lemma~\ref{Alex}.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{DCXSZ}
		Suppose that $F \in C^{2,\nu}_b(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R})$ for some $\nu \in (0,1]$, $T\in (0,1]$, and that
		$\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}, \textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}} \in \mathcal{D}^{\gamma_1,0}_{P_s,0}\big|_{O_{T+s}}$
		such that
		\begin{align*}
			\forall w \in O_{T+s}^{s}: \qquad
			\mathcal{U}^{ 
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
}(w) = \Sigma(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w))
			\quad \text{and} \quad
			\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{ 
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
}(w) = \Sigma(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)).
		\end{align*}
		Then
		\begin{align*}
			&\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_{s}F\big(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}\big) - \mathbf{R}^+_{s}F\big(\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}\big) \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}
			\\&\lesssim
			M_{\nu}\!\left(
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} - \mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}
			\right)
			Q_1\!\left(
			[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ ]_{\overline{{O}_{T+s}^s}},
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)},
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}
			\right),
		\end{align*}
		where
		\begin{align*}
			M_{\nu}(b) &:= \max\{b, b^{\nu}\},\\
			Q_1(b,c,d) &:= \max\Big\{
			(1+b)c+1, b(1+b)^\nu(1+b+c+d),c+c^2+d,(1+b+c)(1+d)
			\Big\},\\
			&\qquad \text{for } b,c,d \geq 0.
		\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
See Appendix \ref{AAzza1}.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
For multivariable polynomials, saying that they are increasing in each argument means that, if all arguments except one are fixed, increasing that argument does not decrease the value of the polynomial.
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{SASewwewewewew}
		Consider the setting of Proposition~\ref{kkkl} with $T=1$ and assume that
		$\Sigma\in C_b^{3,\nu}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R})$ for some $\nu\in(0,1]$.
		Then, for any
		$u_1,u_2\in B_{L^\infty(\mathbb{T}^d)}(u_0,\sigma)$,
		there exists a polynomial $Q_2$, increasing in each of its arguments, such that
		\begin{align}\label{UASJewesdf}
			\begin{split}
				&\lvert\!\lVert 
					\mathbf{R}^+_s \Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_2))
					-\mathbf{R}^+_s \Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_1))
				 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}
				\\
				&\lesssim
				\max\Big\{
				\Vert u_2-u_1\Vert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)},
				\Vert u_2-u_1\Vert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}^{\nu}
				\Big\}
				\\
				&\quad\times
				\mathcal{E}_2\Big(
				\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{s+2}^{s-1}}},
				[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
\ ]_{\overline{O_{s+1}^s}},
				\|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+1}^s}},
				\sup_{\substack{r\in[0,1]\\w\in O_{s+1}^s}}
				\left\|
				\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_1+r(u_2-u_1)}(w)
				\right\|,
				\|u_1\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)},
				\|u_2\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				\Big).
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		where
		\begin{align*}
			\mathcal{E}_2(b,c,d,f,g,h)
			&:=
			\exp\Big(
			Q_2(b,c,d,f,g,h)
			\Big),
		\end{align*}
		for $b,c,d,f,g,h\geq 0$.
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Thanks to Lemma \ref{DCXSZ}, we have
		\begin{align*}
			&\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s \Sigma'( \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_2) )-\mathbf{R}^+_s \Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_1)) \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}
			\\&\lesssim
			M_{\nu}\!\left(
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_2) - \mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_1) \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}
			\right)
			Q_1\!\left(
			[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ ]_{\overline{{O}_{1+s}^s}},
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_1) \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)},
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_2) \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}
			\right),
		\end{align*}
		Thanks to Proposition \ref{kkkl}, we have
		\begin{align*}
			\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_2) - \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_1)=\int_{0}^{1}D_{u_1+r(u_2-u_1)}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[u_2-u_1]\mathrm{d}r
		\end{align*}
		In particular, we have
		\begin{align*}
			\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_2)-\mathbf{R}^+_s \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_1) \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}\leq \int_{0}^{1} \lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s D_{u_1+r(u_2-u_1)}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[u_2-u_1] \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}\mathrm{d}r
		\end{align*}
		The claim now follows from a tedious but straightforward application of Lemma~\ref{Alex}, Lemma~\ref{DCXSZ}, and Corollary~\ref{UJU}.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- proposition-3: body_md -->
\label{UJUJUJU}
		Consider the setting of Corollary \ref{SASewwewewewew}.
		Then there exists a polynomial $Q_3$, increasing in each of its arguments, such that
		\begin{align}\label{I789s}
			\begin{split}
				&\lvert\!\lVert D_{u_2}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}]
					-
					D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}]
				 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{1+s}}^{(s)} \lesssim
				\max\big\{\Vert u_2-u_1\Vert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}, \Vert u_2-u_1\Vert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}^{\nu}\big\} \Vert \tilde{u}\Vert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				\\&\quad \times\mathcal{E}_3\Big(
				\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{s+2}^{s-1}}},
				[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
\ ]_{\overline{O_{s+1}^s}},
				\|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+1}^s}},
				\sup_{\substack{r\in[0,1]\\w\in O_{s+1}^s}}
				\left\|
				\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_1+r(u_2-u_1)}(w)
				\right\|,
				\|u_1\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)},
				\|u_2\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				\Big)
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		where
		\begin{align*}
			\mathcal{E}_3(b,c,d,f,g,h)
			&:=
			\exp\Big(
			Q_3(b,c,d,f,g,h)
			\Big),
		\end{align*}
		for $b,c,d,f,g,h\geq 0$.
<!-- end proposition-3 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
Thanks to Proposition~\ref{kkkl}, we have
		\begin{align*}
			& \mathbf{R}^+_{s}D_{u_2}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}]-\mathbf{R}^+_{s}D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}]
			\\&	=
			\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{0},1-\kappa}
			\Big(
			\big(\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_2)) \star D_{u_2}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}]\big) \tilde{\star}\textcolor{red}{\bullet}-\big(\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_1)) \star D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}]\big)\tilde{\star}\textcolor{red}{\bullet}
			\Big)\\&+\mu\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{1},2}\bigg( \mathbf{R}^+_{s}D_{u_2}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}]-\mathbf{R}^+_{s}D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}]\bigg)\\&=\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{0},1-\kappa}\Bigg(\Bigg(\Big(\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_1))\Big)\star\Big( D_{u_2}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}]-D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}]\Big)\Bigg)\tilde{\star}\textcolor{red}{\bullet}\Bigg)\\&+ \mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{0},1-\kappa}\Bigg(\Bigg(  \Big(\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_2))-\mathbf{R}^+_{s}\Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_1))\Big)\star  D_{u_2}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}]\Bigg)\tilde{\star}\textcolor{red}{\bullet} \Bigg)+\mu\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{1},2}\bigg( D_{u_2}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}]-D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}]\bigg).
		\end{align*}
		Now we can apply Proposition~\ref{onwall} with
		\begin{align*}
			&\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}}=\Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_1)),
			\\&\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{W}}=\bigg(\Big(\Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_2))-\Sigma'(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}(u_1))\Big)\star  D_{u_2}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}]\bigg)\tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
			\\
			&u=0.
		\end{align*}
		First, note that from Remark~\ref{BOUNDED} and Lemma~\ref{Alex},
		\begin{align}\label{ASas7qwqwqwqc}
			\begin{split}
				&\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_s \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{V}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{1+s}^s}^{(s)}\\&\lesssim 	1+\big([ \  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ ]_{\overline{O_{T+s}^{\,s}}}\big)^2
				+
				\bigg( P_{1}\Big(\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{s+2}^{s-1}}},[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
\ ]_{\overline{O_{s+1}^s}},\|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+1}^s}},\sup_{w\in O_{s+1}^s}
				\|\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_1}(w)\|,\|u_1\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}\Big)\bigg)^2.
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		From \eqref{multi} and Lemma \ref{stat},
