			<\infty,
			\qquad \forall p\geq1.
		\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
For \eqref{A75qwewc}, see \cite[Theorems~1 and~3]{CFW26}, valid for
		$\kappa<\bar\kappa\approx0.1321$. An analogous
		a priori estimate in the full subcritical regime is established in
		\cite[Theorem~1.1]{ES26}.
		For the second part, Proposition~\ref{Ujasn785sw} yields, for every
		$p\geq1$,
		\[
		\sup_{\epsilon\in(0,1)}
		\mathbb{E}\Big[
		\Vert\mathcal{Z}^{\epsilon}\Vert_{\overline{O_{2}^{-1}}}^{p}
		\Big]
		<\infty,
		\]
		and
		\begin{align}\label{sadsdsdfsfs}
			\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow0}
			\mathbb{E}\Vert\mathcal Z^\epsilon;\mathcal Z
			\Vert_{\overline{O_{2}^{-1}}}
			=0.
		\end{align}
		Hence, by passing to a subsequence and using Fatou's lemma,
		$\mathcal Z$ has finite moments of every order. Together with
		\eqref{A75qwewc}, this yields \eqref{Asddadvse}.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{METT}
		For $\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty\}$, define
		\[
		F_\lambda(\omega)
		:=
		\left\{
		\xi \in C(\mathbb{T}^d):
		\limsup_{t\to\infty}
		\frac1t
		\log\|\psi_\omega^t (\xi)\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
		\le\lambda
		\right\}.
		\]
		
		Then there exists a $\theta_t$-invariant subset of $\Omega$ of full measure for all $t\in\mathbb{R}$, again denoted by $\Omega$, on which the following assertions hold:
		\begin{enumerate}
			
			\item There exists a decreasing sequence of Lyapunov exponents
			\[
			\lambda_1>\lambda_2>\cdots,
			\]
			or a finite sequence followed by $-\infty$, satisfying
			\[
			\lim_{i\to\infty}\lambda_i=-\infty.
			\]
			
			\item Corresponding to every finite Lyapunov exponent $\lambda_i$, there exists a finite-dimensional invariant subspace
			\[
			H_\omega^i\subset C(\mathbb{T}^d),
			\]
			called the \emph{Oseledets subspace}, such that
			\[
			\psi_\omega^t(H_\omega^i)
			=
			H_{\theta_t\omega}^i,
			\qquad
			t\ge0.
			\]
			
			\item The spaces $H_\omega^i$ determine the filtration
			\[
			F_{\lambda_1}(\omega)=C(\mathbb{T}^d),
			\qquad
			F_{\lambda_i}(\omega)
			=
			H_\omega^i
			\oplus
			F_{\lambda_{i+1}}(\omega),
			\]
			and hence
			\[
			C(\mathbb{T}^d)
			=
			\bigoplus_{j=1}^iH_\omega^j
			\oplus
			F_{\lambda_{i+1}}(\omega)
			\]
			for every $i\ge1$.
			
			\item If $h_\omega\in H_\omega^i\setminus \lbrace 0\rbrace$, then
			\[
			\lim_{t\to\infty}
			\frac1t
			\log
			\|\psi_\omega^t (h_\omega)\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
			=
			\lambda_i,
			\]
			and
			\[
			\lim_{t\to\infty}
			\frac1t
			\log
			\big\|
			(\psi_{\theta_{-t}\omega}^t|_{H^{i}_{\theta_{-t}\omega}})^{-1}
			(h_\omega)
			\big\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
			=
			-\lambda_i.
			\]
		\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
The goal is to apply the multiplicative ergodic theorem for Banach spaces
		stated in \cite[Theorem~1.21]{GVR23A}. We first fix an arbitrary time step
		$t_{0}\in (0,1]$ and obtain the result for the discrete cocycle $(\psi^{nt_0}_{\omega})_{n\geq 0}$. By Lemma~\ref{Asqwqw}, the operator
		$\psi^{t_0}_{\omega}$ is compact for every $\omega\in\Omega$. It remains to
		verify the integrability condition
		\begin{align}\label{ASaickasda}
			\log^{+}\Big(
			\sup_{s\in[0,t_0]}	\|\psi^{s}_{\omega}\|_{\mathcal{L}(C(\mathbb{T}^d),C(\mathbb{T}^d))}
			\Big)
			\in L^{1}(\Omega).
		\end{align}
		To this end, recall that $\psi^{t}_{\omega}=D_{Y_{\omega}}\varphi^{t}_{\omega}$. Thanks to Corollary \ref{UJU}, for each $\tilde{u}\in C(\mathbb{T}^d)$, we have
		\begin{align}\label{TRT1}
			\begin{split}
				&	\sup_{s\in[0,t_0]}\Vert \psi^{s}_{\omega}[\tilde{u}]\Vert\leq \lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_0 D_{u_1}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}[\tilde{u}]  \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,\,0,\,O_{1}}^{(0)}
				\\
				&\qquad\leq
				\mathcal{E}_1\Big(
				\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{2}^{-1}}},
				[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
\ ]_{\overline{O_{1}^0}},
				\|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{1}^0}},
				\sup_{z\in O_{1}^0}
				\|\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{Y_\omega}(z)\|,
				\|Y_\omega\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				\Big)
				\Vert \tilde{u} \Vert_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)},
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		Note that from \eqref{A75qwewc},
		\begin{align}\label{TRT2}
			\begin{split}
				\sup_{z\in O_{1}^0}
				\|\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{Y_\omega}(z)\|&\leq \sup_{s\in [0,1]}\Vert\varphi^{s}_{\omega}(Y_\omega)\Vert_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}=\sup_{s\in [0,1]}\Vert Y_{\theta_s\omega}\Vert_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}\\&\leq C_\mu
				\max\left\{
				\|Y_\omega\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)},
				P_0\left(
				\|\Pi\|_{\overline{O_{1}^0}},
				[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
\ ]_{\overline{O_{1}^0}}
				\right)
				\right\}\in\bigcap_{p\geq 1}L^{p}(\Omega).
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		With an argument analogous to that in the proof of Lemma \ref{ASasd78axx}, we have
		\begin{align}\label{TRT3}
			\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{2}^{-1}}},
			[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
\ ]_{\overline{O_{1}^0}},
			\|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{1}^0}}\in\bigcap_{p\geq 1}L^{p}(\Omega)
		\end{align}
		Now thanks to the definition of the function $\mathcal{E}_1$ in Corollary \ref{UJU} and together with \eqref{TRT1}-\eqref{TRT3}, one can indeed derive \eqref{ASaickasda}. Thus, we verify all the criteria needed for the requirement of \cite[Theorem~1.21]{GVR23A}. And so the multiplicative ergodic theorem holds for the discrete cocycle $(\psi^{nt_0}_{\omega})_{n\geq 0}$. To obtain the continuous version, it is sufficient to check that by doing an analogous argument,
		\begin{align*}
			\log^{+}\Big(
			\sup_{s\in[0,t_0]}	\|\psi^{t_0-s}_{\theta_s\omega}\|_{\mathcal{L}(C(\mathbb{T}^d),C(\mathbb{T}^d))}
			\Big)
			\in L^{1}(\Omega).
		\end{align*}
		Then, by a standard adaptation of the arguments in \cite[Theorem 3.3 and Lemma 3.4]{LL10}, one can obtain the continuous-time version of the multiplicative ergodic theorem from the discrete case.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- definition-13: body_md -->
\label{ASAqvfdg}
		Consider the setting of Theorem~\ref{METT}. We set, whenever the corresponding spaces are well defined,
		\begin{align}\label{linn}
			S_{\omega}
			:= F_{\lambda^-}(\omega),\quad
			U_{\omega}
			:= \bigoplus_{i:\lambda_i>0}H_{\omega}^{i},\quad
			C_{\omega}
			&:= H^{i_c}_{\omega},
		\end{align}
		where 	$$
		\lambda^- := \sup\{\lambda_i:\lambda_i<0\},
		\qquad
		\lambda_{i_c}=0.
		$$
<!-- end definition-13 -->
<!-- remark-19: body_md -->
Let
	\[
	C^{1-\kappa-\epsilon}_0(\mathbb T^d)
	:=
	\overline{C^\infty(\mathbb T^d)}^{\,C^{1-\kappa-\epsilon}}
	\]
	denote the little Hölder space, which is separable. By
	Lemma~\ref{Asqwqw}, the cocycle \(\psi\) also acts on
	\(C^{1-\kappa-\epsilon}_0(\mathbb T^d)\), and hence the Multiplicative
	Ergodic Theorem can be applied on this space. Moreover, the Lyapunov
	exponents and, under the natural embedding into \(C(\mathbb T^d)\), the
	corresponding Oseledets subspaces coincide with those obtained on
	\(C(\mathbb T^d)\); see~\cite[Theorems~5.17 and~5.18]{BGS2025}.
<!-- end remark-19 -->
<!-- remark-20: body_md -->
There is a rich literature on the Multiplicative Ergodic Theorem; see, e.g., \cite{LL10,GTQ15,Blu16}. The formulation developed in \cite{GVRS22,GVR23A}, which we use in this manuscript, is particularly flexible and well suited to applications, especially when verifying the assumptions of the theorem.
<!-- end remark-20 -->
<!-- proposition-6: body_md -->
\label{FIRTA}
		There exists an increasing $C^1$ function $h:\mathbb{R}_{+}\to \mathbb{R}_{+}$ and a measurable function $R:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}_+$ such that, for each $u_1,u_2\in C(\mathbb{T}^d)$,
		\begin{align*}
			\Vert u_i\Vert_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}\leq 1 , \ \  i=1,2 ,
		\end{align*}
		we have
		\begin{align}
			\begin{split}
				&\sup_{s\in[0,1]}
				\left\|
				\varphi^s_\omega(u_2+Y_\omega)
				-\varphi^s_\omega(u_1+Y_\omega)
				-\psi^s_\omega[u_2-u_1]
				\right\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				\\
				&\qquad\leq
				\|u_2-u_1\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				\Big(
				\|u_1\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}^\nu
				+
				\|u_2\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}^\nu
				\Big)
				h\Big(
				\|u_1\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				+
				\|u_2\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				\Big)
				R(\omega);
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		Also,
		\begin{align*}
			\lim_{t\to\infty}
			\frac{1}{t}\log^+R(\theta_t\omega)=0
			\qquad\text{a.s.}
		\end{align*}
		In particular, the first-order approximation condition holds.
<!-- end proposition-6 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
For $\tau \in [0,1]$, let
		\begin{align*}
			L^{\tau}_{Y_\omega}(u_1,u_2):=u_1+Y_\omega+\tau (u_2-u_1),
		\end{align*}
		then we have
		\begin{align*}
			\varphi^s_\omega(u_2+Y_\omega)
			-\varphi^s_\omega(u_1+Y_\omega)
			-\psi^s_\omega(u_2-u_1)=\int_{0}^{1}\big[D_{L^{\tau}_{Y_\omega}(u_1,u_2)}\varphi^{s}_{\omega}-D_{Y_{\omega}}\varphi_{\omega}^s\big][u_2-u_1]\mathrm{d}\tau.
		\end{align*}
		Thanks to Proposition \ref{UJUJUJU} 
		\begin{align}\label{I7895454s}
			\begin{split}
				&\sup_{\tau\in [0,1]}\sup_{s\in [0,1]} \big\Vert\big[D_{u_1+Y_\omega+\tau (u_2-u_1)}\varphi^{s}_{\omega}-D_{Y_{\omega}}\varphi^s_\omega\big][u_2-u_1]\big\Vert_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}\leq \\&\sup_{\tau\in [0,1]}\lvert\!\lVert D_{L^{\tau}_{Y_\omega}(u_1,u_2)}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}\big[u_2-u_1\big]
					-
					D_{Y_\omega}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{J}_1}\big[u_2-u_1\big]
				 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{1}}^{(0)} \\&\lesssim
				\sup_{\tau \in [0,1]}\max\big\{\Vert u_1+\tau (u_2-u_1)\Vert_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}, \Vert  u_1+\tau (u_2-u_1)\Vert_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}^{\nu}\big\} \Vert u_2-u_1\Vert_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				\\& \times\sup_{\tau \in [0,1]}\mathcal{E}_3\Big(
				\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{2}^{-1}}},
				[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
\ ]_{\overline{O_{1}^0}},
				\|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{1}^0}},\sup_{\substack{r\in[0,1]\\w\in O_{1}^0}}
				\left\|
				\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{Y_{\omega}+ru_1+r\tau(u_2-u_1)}(w)
				\right\|,
				\|Y_{\omega}\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)},
				\|L^{\tau}_{Y_\omega}(u_1,u_2)\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				\Big)\\&\lesssim \big(\Vert u_1\Vert^{\nu}_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}+\Vert u_2\Vert^{\nu}_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}\big)\Vert u_2-u_1\Vert_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}\big(1+\Vert u_1\Vert_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}+\Vert u_2\Vert_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}\big)\\& \times\sup_{\tau \in [0,1]}\mathcal{E}_3\Big(
				\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{2}^{-1}}},
				[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
\ ]_{\overline{O_{1}^0}},
				\|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{1}^0}},\sup_{\substack{r\in[0,1]\\w\in O_{1}^0}}
				\left\|
				\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{Y_{\omega}+ru_1+r\tau(u_2-u_1)}(w)
				\right\|,
				\|Y_{\omega}\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)},
				\|L^{\tau}_{Y_\omega}(u_1,u_2)\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				\Big)
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		Note that from \eqref{A75qwewc}, for a polynomial $P_0$
		\begin{align}\label{TRT2aaa}
			\begin{split}
				&	\sup_{\substack{r,\tau\in[0,1]\\ z\in O_1^0}}
				\left\|
				\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{Y_\omega+r u_1+r\tau(u_2-u_1)}(z)
				\right\|\leq 	\sup_{\substack{r,\tau\in[0,1]\\ s\in[0,1]}}
				\left\|
				\varphi_\omega^s
				\big(
				Y_\omega+r u_1+r\tau(u_2-u_1)
				\big)
				\right\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}\\&\lesssim 
				\max\bigg\{
				\|Y_\omega\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}+\|u_1\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}+\|u_2\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)},
				P_0\left(
				\|\Pi\|_{\overline{O_{1}^0}},
				[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
\ ]_{\overline{O_{1}^0}}
				\right)
				\bigg\}
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		Recall that for a polynomial $Q_3$ which is increasing in its argument, we have
		\begin{align*}
			\mathcal{E}_3(b,c,d,f,g,h)
			&:=
			\exp\Big(
			Q_3(b,c,d,f,g,h)
			\Big),
		\end{align*}
		Consequently, using \eqref{TRT2}, \eqref{TRT2aaa}, and \eqref{I7895454s}, together with some straightforward algebraic and tedious calculations, we can conclude that one can obtain an increasing $C^1$ function $h:\mathbb{R}_+\to\mathbb{R}_+$ and a polynomial $Q_4$ such that 
		\begin{align}
			\begin{split}
				&\sup_{s\in [0,1]} \big\Vert \varphi^s_\omega(u_2+Y_\omega)
				-\varphi^s_\omega(u_1+Y_\omega)
				-\psi^s_\omega(u_2-u_1)\big\Vert\\&\leq
				\|u_2-u_1\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}\big(\Vert u_1\Vert^{\nu}_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}+\Vert u_2\Vert^{\nu}_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}\big) h\big(\Vert u_1\Vert_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}+\Vert u_2\Vert_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}\big)\\
				&\times \underbrace{\exp\bigg(Q_{4}\big(	\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{2}^{-1}}},
					[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
\ ]_{\overline{O_{1}^0}},
					\|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{1}^0}},\|Y_\omega\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}\big)\bigg)}_{=R(\omega)}
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		The final step is to verify \eqref{RTRTR}. By \eqref{AS89asdxa}, \eqref{TRT2}, and the same argument as in \eqref{TRT3}, we have
		$$
		\log^+\Big(\sup_{s\in[0,1]}R(\theta_s\omega)\Big)\in L^1(\Omega).
		$$
		Hence, Remark~\ref{IOpasasasas} yields \eqref{RTRTR}, and therefore the first-order approximation condition holds.
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{stable_manifold}
		Consider the situation of Theorem \ref{METT}. Let $\lambda(s)\coloneqq \sup\{\lambda_j:\lambda_j<0\}$ and fix an arbitrary time step $t_0\in (0,1]$.
		Then, for every
		\[
		0<\upsilon<-\lambda(s),
		\]
	there exists a family of immersed submanifolds
		\[
		S^\upsilon_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)\subset C(\mathbb{T}^d)
		\]
		and a set $\widetilde{\Omega}\subset\Omega$ of full measure which is $\theta_{t_0}$-invariant and such that the following properties hold:
		\begin{enumerate}
			
			\item There exist random variables 
			\[
			\rho_1^\upsilon(\omega),\rho_2^\upsilon(\omega),
			\]
			which are positive and finite on $\widetilde{\Omega}$, satisfying
			\begin{align}\label{eqn:rho_temp}
				\liminf_{p\to\infty}\frac1p
				\log\rho_i^\upsilon(\theta_{pt_0}\omega)\geq0,
				\qquad i=1,2,
			\end{align}
			such that
			\begin{align}\label{invari}
				\begin{split}
					&\left\{
					u\in C(\mathbb{T}^d):
					\sup_{n\geq0}e^{nt_0\upsilon}
					\left\|\varphi^{nt_0}_{\omega}(u)-Y_{\theta_{nt_0}\omega}\right\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
					<\rho_1^\upsilon(\omega)
					\right\}\subseteq
					S^\upsilon_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)
					\\
					&\qquad\subseteq
					\left\{
					u\in C(\mathbb{T}^d):
					\sup_{n\geq0}e^{nt_0\upsilon}
					\left\|\varphi^{nt_0}_{\omega}(u)-Y_{\theta_{nt_0}\omega}\right\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
					<\rho_2^\upsilon(\omega)
					\right\}.
				\end{split}
			\end{align}
			
			\item The tangent space of $S^\upsilon_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)$ at the stationary point $Y_\omega$ is given by
			\[
			T_{Y_\omega}S^\upsilon_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)
			=
			F_{\lambda(s)}(\omega).
			\]
			
			\item There exists a random variable $N(\omega)$ such that, for all 
			$n\geq N(\omega)$,
			\[
			\varphi^{nt_0}_{\omega}
			\left(S^\upsilon_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)\right)
			\subseteq
			S^\upsilon_{\mathrm{loc}}(\theta_{nt_0}\omega).
			\]
			
			\item \label{aaaa} For
			\[
			0<\upsilon_1\leq\upsilon_2<-\lambda(s),
			\]
			we have
			\[
			S^{\upsilon_2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)
			\subseteq
			S^{\upsilon_1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega),
			\]
			and, for $n\geq N(\omega)$,
			\[
			\varphi^{nt_0}_{\omega}
			\left(S^{\upsilon_1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)\right)
			\subseteq
			S^{\upsilon_2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\theta_{nt_0}\omega).
			\]
			Consequently, for every 
			$u\in S^\upsilon_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)$,
			\begin{align}\label{}
				\limsup_{n\to\infty}
				\frac1n
				\log
				\left\|
				\varphi^{nt_0}_{\omega}(u)-Y_{\theta_{nt_0}\omega}
				\right\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				\leq
				t_0\lambda(s).
			\end{align}
			
			\item We have
			\begin{align}\label{78421215}
\begin{split}
	\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac1n
\log\Bigg[
\sup\Bigg\{
\frac{
	\left\|
	\varphi^{nt_0}_{\omega}(u)
	-\varphi^{nt_0}_{\omega}(\tilde{u})
	\right\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
}{
	\|u-\tilde{u}\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
}:
\ u\neq\tilde{u},\
u,\tilde{u}\in S^\upsilon_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)
\Bigg\}
\Bigg]
\leq
t_0\lambda(s).
\end{split}
			\end{align}
		\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
We aim to apply \cite[Theorem 2.10]{GVR23A}. The requirements of this result are provided by Theorem \ref{METT} and Proposition \ref{FIRTA}.
<!-- end proof-30 -->
<!-- remark-21: body_md -->
The stable manifold theorem has several important roles. One of those that we discuss in this paper concerns local stability. This result is also important for proving synchronization. Note that the stable manifold always exists, since the Lyapunov exponents converge to $-\infty$.
<!-- end remark-21 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
\label{unstable}
		Consider the situation of Theorem \ref{METT} and suppose that 
		$\lambda_1>0$. Define
		\[
		\lambda(u)\coloneqq \inf\{\lambda_i:\lambda_i>0\}.
		\]
		Fix an arbitrary time step $t_{0}\in (0,1]$. Then, for every
		\[
		0<\upsilon<\lambda(u),
		\]
		there exists a family of immersed submanifolds
		\[
		U^\upsilon_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)\subset C(\mathbb{T}^d)
		\]
		and a set $\widetilde{\Omega}\subset\Omega$ of full measure which is $\theta_{t_0}$-invariant such that the following properties hold.	
		\begin{enumerate}
			
			\item There exist random variables 
			\[
			\widetilde{\rho}_1^\upsilon(\omega),
			\widetilde{\rho}_2^\upsilon(\omega),
			\]
			which are positive and finite on $\widetilde{\Omega}$ and satisfy
			\[
			\liminf_{p\to\infty}
			\frac1p
			\log
			\widetilde{\rho}_i^\upsilon(\theta_{-pt_0}\omega)
			\geq0,
			\qquad i=1,2.
			\]
			Moreover,
			\begin{align*}
				&\Bigg\{
				u_\omega\in C(\mathbb{T}^d):
				\exists\{u_{\theta_{-nt_0}\omega}\}_{n\geq1}
				\text{ such that }
				\varphi^{mt_0}_{\theta_{-nt_0}\omega}
				(u_{\theta_{-nt_0}\omega})
				=
				u_{\theta_{(m-n)t_0}\omega},
				\quad 0\leq m\leq n,
				\\
				&\qquad
				\sup_{n\geq0}
				e^{nt_0\upsilon}
				\left\|
				u_{\theta_{-nt_0}\omega}
				-
				Y_{\theta_{-nt_0}\omega}
				\right\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				<
				\widetilde{\rho}_1^\upsilon(\omega)
				\Bigg\}
				\\
				&\qquad\subseteq
				U^\upsilon_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)
				\subseteq
				\Bigg\{
				u_\omega\in C(\mathbb{T}^d):
				\exists\{u_{\theta_{-nt_0}\omega}\}_{n\geq1}
				\text{ such that }
				\varphi^{mt_0}_{\theta_{-nt_0}\omega}
				(u_{\theta_{-nt_0}\omega})
				=
				u_{\theta_{(m-n)t_0}\omega},
				\quad 0\leq m\leq n,
				\\
				&\qquad
				\sup_{n\geq0}
				e^{nt_0\upsilon}
				\left\|
				u_{\theta_{-nt_0}\omega}
				-
				Y_{\theta_{-nt_0}\omega}
				\right\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				<
				\widetilde{\rho}_2^\upsilon(\omega)
				\Bigg\}.
			\end{align*}
			
			\item The tangent space of the unstable manifold is given by
			\[
			T_{Y_\omega}U^\upsilon_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)
			=
			\bigoplus_{\lambda_i>0}H^i_\omega .
			\]
			
			\item There exists a random variable $N(\omega)$ such that, for every 
			$n\geq N(\omega)$,
			\[
			U^\upsilon_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)
			\subseteq
			\varphi^{nt_0}_{\theta_{-nt_0}\omega}
			\left(
			U^\upsilon_{\mathrm{loc}}(\theta_{-nt_0}\omega)
			\right).
			\]
			
			\item For
			\[
			0<\upsilon_1\leq\upsilon_2<\lambda(u),
			\]
			we have
			\[
			U^{\upsilon_2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)
			\subseteq
			U^{\upsilon_1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega).
			\]
			Moreover, for $n\geq N(\omega)$,
			\[
			U^{\upsilon_1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)
			\subseteq
			\varphi^{nt_0}_{\theta_{-nt_0}\omega}
			\left(
			U^{\upsilon_2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\theta_{-nt_0}\omega)
			\right).
			\]
			Consequently, for every
			$u_\omega\in U^\upsilon_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)$,
			\begin{align}\label{eqn:contr_char_1}
				\limsup_{n\to\infty}
				\frac1n
				\log
				\left\|
				u_{\theta_{-nt_0}\omega}
				-
				Y_{\theta_{-nt_0}\omega}
				\right\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				\leq
				-t_0\lambda(u).
			\end{align}
			
			\item We have
			\begin{align*}
				\limsup_{n\to\infty}
				\frac1n
				\log
				\Bigg[
				\sup
				\Bigg\{
				\frac{
					\left\|
					u_{\theta_{-nt_0}\omega}
					-
					\tilde{u}_{\theta_{-nt_0}\omega}
					\right\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				}{
					\left\|
					u_\omega-\tilde{u}_\omega
					\right\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
				}
				:
				u_\omega\neq\tilde{u}_\omega,\ 
				u_\omega,\tilde{u}_\omega
				\in U^\upsilon_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)
				\Bigg\}
				\Bigg]
				\leq
				-t_0\lambda(u).
			\end{align*}
			
		\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- proof-31: body_md -->
The result follows directly from \cite[Theorem~2.17]{GVR23A}, whose requirements are provided by Theorem \ref{METT} and Proposition \ref{FIRTA}.
<!-- end proof-31 -->
<!-- remark-22: body_md -->
The unstable manifold describes the directions along which trajectories diverge from the stationary point in forward time. In particular, it characterizes the local behavior of trajectories that originate near the stationary point and move away from it. This provides a natural connection to the global dynamics and, in particular, to the possible attraction toward other invariant sets or attractors.
<!-- end remark-22 -->
<!-- remark-23: body_md -->
\label{CISa}
		In the statements of Theorems~\ref{stable_manifold} and \ref{unstable}, we
		first discretize the cocycle and then state the corresponding results for the
		resulting discrete-time cocycle. This shows that the invariant manifold is eventually
		invariant under the discrete-time cocycle. A natural question is whether this
		invariance property also holds for the continuous-time cocycle. In fact, we
		obtain a slightly weaker result: for every
		$0<\upsilon_1<-\lambda(s)$, on a set of full measure, there exists a time
		$t(\omega)>0$ such that, for all $t>t(\omega)$,
		\[
		\varphi^{t}_{\omega}
		\big(S^{\upsilon_1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)\big)
		\subset
		S^{\upsilon_1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\theta_t\omega).
		\]	
		We sketch the main idea. Indeed, let $t\geq0$ and write
		\[
		t=m_t t_0+r,
		\qquad
		m_t:=\left\lfloor\frac{t}{t_0}\right\rfloor\in\mathbb{N}_0,
		\qquad
		0\leq r<t_0.
		\]
		Let
		\[
		0<\upsilon_1<\upsilon_2<-\lambda(s)
		\]
		and let $u\in S^{\upsilon_1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)$. Then
		\begin{align}\label{Ujmas89qwz}
			\begin{split}
				&\varphi^{t}_{\omega}(u)-Y_{\theta_t\omega}
				\\
				&\quad=
				\varphi^{r}_{\theta_{m_t t_0}\omega}
				\big(
				\varphi^{m_t t_0}_{\omega}(u)
				\big)
				-
				\varphi^{r}_{\theta_{m_t t_0}\omega}
				\big(
				Y_{\theta_{m_t t_0}\omega}
				\big)
				\\
				&\quad=
				\int_0^1
				D_{\alpha\varphi^{m_t t_0}_{\omega}(u)
					+(1-\alpha)Y_{\theta_{m_t t_0}\omega}}
				\varphi^{r}_{\theta_{m_t t_0}\omega}
				\Big[
				\varphi^{m_t t_0}_{\omega}(u)
				-
				Y_{\theta_{m_t t_0}\omega}
				\Big]
				\,\mathrm{d}\alpha .
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		By Theorem~\ref{stable_manifold}, Item~\ref{aaaa}, for all sufficiently
		large $t$,
		\begin{align}\label{sdf9sfsad}
			\varphi^{m_t t_0}_{\omega}
			\left(S^{\upsilon_1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)\right)
			\subseteq
			S^{\upsilon_2}_{\mathrm{loc}}
			(\theta_{m_t t_0}\omega).
		\end{align}
	Moreover, by \eqref{invari} together with \eqref{78421215}, the convergence
	\[
	\varphi^{mt_0}_{\omega}(u)
	\longrightarrow
	Y_{\theta_{mt_0}\omega}
	\]
	is exponentially fast uniformly in
	$u\in S^{\upsilon_1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega)$. Therefore, by Corollary~\ref{UJU},
		Remark~\ref{sds78asdaad}, and the estimates established above, we obtain
		\begin{align}\label{Y78azswds}
			\lim_{m\to\infty}
			\frac{1}{m}
	\sup_{\substack{
			u\in S_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\upsilon_1}(\omega)\\
			0\leq\alpha\leq1\\
			0\leq r\leq t_0}}
			\log^+
			\left\|
			D_{\alpha\varphi^{mt_0}_{\omega}(u)
				+(1-\alpha)Y_{\theta_{mt_0}\omega}}
			\varphi^{r}_{\theta_{mt_0}\omega}
			\right\|_{\mathcal{L}(C(\mathbb{T}^d),C(\mathbb{T}^d))}
			=0.
		\end{align}
		Applying \eqref{Ujmas89qwz} with $m_t$ replaced by $m_t+n$, together with \eqref{Y78azswds}, yields the required estimate uniformly in $n\geq0$. Hence, by \eqref{invari},
		$$
		\varphi_\omega^t(u)
		\in S_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\upsilon_1}(\theta_t\omega)
		$$	
		for all sufficiently large $t$. An analogous argument applies to the unstable manifold in Theorem~\ref{unstable}.
<!-- end remark-23 -->
<!-- theorem-8: body_md -->
\label{center}
		In Theorem~\ref{METT}, suppose that, for  $ i_c\geq 1$,
		we have $\lambda_{i_c}=0$. Fix an arbitrary time step $t_0>0$ and assume
		\[
		0 < \nu < \min\!\bigl\lbrace \lambda_{i_c-1},\, -\lambda_{i_c+1}\bigr\rbrace,
		\]
		with the convention that $\lambda_0=\infty$ if $i_c=1$.
		Let
		\[
		\mathbb{N}_0t_0
		=
		\bigl\{nt_0:n\in\mathbb{N}_0=\mathbb{N}\cup\lbrace0\rbrace\bigr\}.
		\]
		Then there exist a $\theta_{t_0}$-invariant subset
		$\tilde{\Omega}\subseteq\Omega$ of full measure, a continuous cocycle
		\[
		\bar{\varphi} \colon \mathbb{N}_0 t_0 \times \tilde{\Omega} \times C(\mathbb{T}^d) \to C(\mathbb{T}^d) ,
		\]
		and a positive random variable
		$\rho^c \colon \tilde{\Omega} \to (0,\infty)$ such that
		\[
		\liminf_{n\to\pm\infty}
		\frac{1}{|n|}\log\rho^c\!\bigl(\theta_{nt_0}\omega\bigr)\geq0.
		\]
		Moreover, the functions $\rho^c$ and $\bar{\varphi}$ satisfy
		\[
		\|u\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}\leq\rho^c(\omega)
		\implies
		\bar{\varphi}^{t_0}_\omega(Y_\omega+u)
		=
		\varphi^{t_0}_\omega(Y_\omega+u).
		\]
		In addition, for every $\omega\in\tilde{\Omega}$, there exists a function
		\[
		h^c_\omega\colon C_\omega\to\mathcal{M}^{c,\nu}_\omega\subset C(\mathbb{T}^d),
		\]
		such that:
		\begin{itemize}
			\item[(i)] $h^c_\omega$ is a Lipschitz homeomorphism with
			$$
			h^c_\omega(0)=Y_\omega,
			$$
			and is differentiable at zero.
			\item[(ii)] $\mathcal{M}^{c,\nu}_\omega$ is a topological Banach manifold\footnote{That is, $\mathcal{M}^{c,\nu}_\omega$ is locally homeomorphic to an open subset of the Banach space $C_\omega$.} modeled on $C_\omega$.
			
			
			\item[(iii)] $\mathcal{M}^{c,\nu}_\omega$ is
			$\bar{\varphi}$-invariant; that is, for every $n\in\mathbb{N}_0$,
			\[
			\bar{\varphi}^{nt_0}_\omega
			\bigl(\mathcal{M}^{c,\nu}_\omega\bigr)
			\subseteq
			\mathcal{M}^{c,\nu}_{\theta_{nt_0}\omega}.
			\]
			
			\item[(iv)] For every
			$u_\omega\in\mathcal{M}^{c,\nu}_\omega$, there exists a sequence
			$\{u_{\theta_{-nt_0}\omega}\}_{n\geq1}$ such that, if we define
			\[
			j\leq0:\qquad
			\bar{\varphi}^{jt_0}_\omega(u_\omega)
			:=u_{\theta_{jt_0}\omega},
			\]
			then
			\[
			\forall(k,j)\in\mathbb{N}_0\times\mathbb{Z}:\qquad
			\bar{\varphi}^{kt_0}_{\theta_{jt_0}\omega}
			\bigl(\bar{\varphi}^{jt_0}_\omega(u_\omega)\bigr)
			=
			\bar{\varphi}^{(k+j)t_0}_\omega(u_\omega).
			\]
			Moreover,
			\[
			\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}
			\exp(-\nu t_0|j|)
			\left\|
			\bar{\varphi}^{jt_0}_\omega(u_\omega)
			-
			Y_{\theta_{jt_0}\omega}
			\right\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
			<\infty.
			\]
		\end{itemize}
<!-- end theorem-8 -->
<!-- proof-32: body_md -->
The result follows directly from \cite[Theorem~3.14]{GVR25C}, with the same requirements as for the stable and unstable manifold results, i.e., Theorem \ref{METT} and Proposition \ref{FIRTA}.
<!-- end proof-32 -->
<!-- lemma-18: body_md -->
\label{ASasxassas}
	Consider the setting of Theorem~\ref{stable_manifold}. Assume that
	\[
	\lambda(s)=\lambda_1<0,
	\]
	and fix an arbitrary time step $t_0\in(0,1]$. Then, for every
	\[
	0<\upsilon<-\lambda_1,
	\]
	there exists a set of full measure $\widetilde{\Omega}$ and a positive random variable $\rho_3^\upsilon$ satisfying
	\begin{align}\label{eqn:rho_tempa}
		\liminf_{p\to\infty}\frac1p
		\log\rho_3^\upsilon(\theta_{pt_0}\omega)\geq0,
	\end{align}
	such that, for every $\omega\in\widetilde{\Omega}$,
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item
		\begin{align}\label{asaswqew}
			B_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}\bigl(Y_\omega,\rho_3^\upsilon(\omega)\bigr)
			\subseteq
			S^\upsilon_{\mathrm{loc}}(\omega).
		\end{align}
		
		\item We have
		\begin{align}\label{eqn:local_contraction}
			\limsup_{t\to\infty}
			\frac1t
			\log
			\left[
			\sup_{\substack{
					u\neq\tilde u\\
					u,\tilde u\in
					B_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}(Y_\omega,\rho_3^\upsilon(\omega))}}
			\frac{
				\left\|
				\varphi^t_\omega(u)
				-\varphi^t_\omega(\tilde u)
				\right\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
			}{
				\|u-\tilde u\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
			}
			\right]
			\leq
			\lambda_1,
		\end{align}
Consequently
		\begin{align}\label{eqn:contr_char6}
			\limsup_{t\to\infty}
			\frac1t
			\log
			\left[
			\sup_{u\in B_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}(Y_\omega,\rho_3^\upsilon(\omega))}
			\left\|
			\varphi^{t}_{\omega}(u)-Y_{\theta_t\omega}
			\right\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)}
			\right]
			\leq
			\lambda_1.
		\end{align}
	\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-18 -->
<!-- proof-33: body_md -->
The proof essentially follows from Theorem~\ref{stable_manifold} and Remark~\ref{CISa}. Indeed, since $\lambda_1<0$ is the top Lyapunov exponent, we have
	\[
	F_{\lambda_1}(\omega)=C(\mathbb{T}^d).
	\]
	Then, by following the proof of \cite[Theorem~2.10]{GVR23A}, one obtains a positive random variable $\rho_3^\upsilon$ satisfying \eqref{eqn:rho_tempa} such that \eqref{asaswqew} holds. The second assertion follows from Theorem~\ref{stable_manifold} together with Remark~\ref{CISa}.
<!-- end proof-33 -->
<!-- theorem-9: body_md -->
\label{stabi}
	Consider the SPDE
	\begin{align}\label{MAINAAssAA}
		\begin{cases}
			(\partial_t-\Delta)u^{\delta}
			=
			\mu u^{\delta}
			+\delta\Sigma(u^\delta)\,\xi^Q,
			& t>0,\\
			u^\delta(0,\cdot)=u_0\in L^\infty(\mathbb T^d),
		\end{cases}
	\end{align}
	where $\Sigma(0)=0$, $\mu<0$, and $\delta>0$. Then, for $\delta>0$ sufficiently small, the top Lyapunov exponent $\lambda_1^\delta$ is negative. In particular, the conclusions of Lemma~\ref{ASasxassas} hold with $Y_\omega=0$.
<!-- end theorem-9 -->
<!-- proof-34: body_md -->
Thanks to Lemma~\ref{ASasxassas}, it remains to prove that the top Lyapunov exponent is negative. Let
	\[
	\psi^{t,\delta}_\omega:=D_0\varphi^{t,\delta}_\omega .
	\]
	By Theorem~\ref{METT}, for almost every $\omega$ there exists
	\[
	u_\omega\in H^1_\omega\setminus\{0\}
	\]
	such that
	\begin{align}\label{Ikaswwe25}
		\lambda_1^\delta
		=
		\lim_{m\to\infty}
		\frac{1}{mt_0}
		\log
		\left\|
		\psi^{mt_0,\delta}_\omega(u_\omega)
		\right\|_{C(\mathbb{T}^d)} .
	\end{align}
	Moreover, by the cocycle property,
	\begin{align*}
		\frac{1}{knt_0}
		\log
		\left\|
		\psi^{knt_0,\delta}_\omega
		\right\|_{\mathcal L(C(\mathbb{T}^d),C(\mathbb{T}^d))}
		\leq
		\frac1k
		\sum_{0\leq j<k}
		\frac{
			\log
			\left\|
			\psi^{nt_0,\delta}_{\theta_{jnt_0}\omega}
			\right\|_{\mathcal L(C(\mathbb{T}^d),C(\mathbb{T}^d))}
		}{nt_0}.
	\end{align*}
	Hence, by the Birkhoff ergodic theorem and the ergodicity of
	$\theta_{nt_0}$,
	\begin{align*}
		\limsup_{k\to\infty}
		\frac{1}{knt_0}
		\log
		\left\|
		\psi^{knt_0,\delta}_\omega
		\right\|_{\mathcal L(C(\mathbb{T}^d),C(\mathbb{T}^d))}
		\leq
		\frac{1}{nt_0}
		\mathbb{E}\left[
		\log
		\left\|
		\psi^{nt_0,\delta}_\omega
		\right\|_{\mathcal L(C(\mathbb{T}^d),C(\mathbb{T}^d))}
		\right],
		\qquad \mathbb P\text{-a.s.}
	\end{align*}
	Together with \eqref{Ikaswwe25}, this yields
	\begin{align}\label{Kaser}
		\lambda_1^\delta
		\leq
		\inf_{n\geq1}
		\frac{1}{nt_0}
		\mathbb{E}\left[
		\log
		\left\|
		\psi^{nt_0,\delta}_\omega
		\right\|_{\mathcal L(C(\mathbb{T}^d),C(\mathbb{T}^d))}
		\right].
	\end{align}
	We now use \eqref{Kaser} to show that $\lambda_1^\delta<0$ for sufficiently small $\delta$. By Proposition~\ref{kkkl}, the linearized equation at the stationary solution $0$ is
	\begin{align}\label{MAINAAssAAsasd}
		\begin{cases}
			(\partial_t-\Delta)v^\delta
			=
			\mu v^\delta
			+\delta\Sigma'(0)v^\delta\,\xi^Q,
			& t>0,\\
			v^\delta(0,\cdot)=v_0\in C(\mathbb{T}^d),
		\end{cases}
	\end{align}
	where the solution is understood in the sense of Definition~\ref{dfn}.
	Let
	\[
	v^\delta(t,x)=e^{\mu t}\overline v^\delta(t,x).
	\]
	Then, by the properties of the reconstruction operator, $\overline v^\delta$ solves
	\begin{align}\label{MAINAAssAAsassdd}
		\begin{cases}
			(\partial_t-\Delta)\overline v^\delta
			=
			\delta\Sigma'(0)\overline v^\delta\,\xi^Q,
			& t>0,\\
			\overline v^\delta(0,\cdot)=v_0\in C(\mathbb{T}^d).
		\end{cases}
	\end{align}
	Consequently,
	\[
	\psi^{t,\delta}_\omega
	=
	e^{\mu t}\overline\psi^{t,\delta}_\omega,
	\]
	where $\overline\psi^{t,\delta}$ denotes the cocycle associated with
	\eqref{MAINAAssAAsassdd}. Thus, by \eqref{Kaser},
	\begin{align}\label{small-noise-Lyap}
		\lambda_1^\delta
		\leq
		\mu
		+
		\frac{1}{t_0}
		\mathbb{E}\left[
		\log
		\left\|
		\overline\psi^{t_0,\delta}_\omega
		\right\|_{\mathcal L(C(\mathbb{T}^d),C(\mathbb{T}^d))}
		\right].
	\end{align}
Regarding \eqref{MAINAAssAAsassdd} as an equation driven by the corresponding model scaled by $\delta$, the stability of the solution with respect to the model, together with Proposition~\ref{onwall} and the dominated convergence theorem, yields
\[
\lim_{\delta\to0}
\frac{1}{t_0}
\mathbb{E}\left[
\log
\left\|
\overline\psi^{t_0,\delta}_\omega
\right\|_{\mathcal L(C(\mathbb{T}^d),C(\mathbb{T}^d))}
\right]
=
\frac{1}{t_0}
\log
\left\|
e^{t_0\Delta}
\right\|_{\mathcal L(C(\mathbb{T}^d),C(\mathbb{T}^d))}
=0.
\]
	Since $\mu<0$, \eqref{small-noise-Lyap} implies that, for $\delta>0$
	sufficiently small,
	\[
	\lambda_1^\delta<0.
	\]
<!-- end proof-34 -->
<!-- proof-35: body_md -->
\label{Alexi}
		Some parts of the proof are similar to \cite[Lemmas~2 and~3 and Theorem~4]{CFW26}.
		The main difference is that we formulate the argument within the framework of
		\cite{Hai14}. In particular, we use the Schauder-type estimates established in
		\cite{Hai14}. Moreover, we employ a patching argument to extend the local
		estimates. First, recall that for
		$w\in O^s_{s+T}$, 
		\begin{align*}
			\left(\mathbf{R}^+_s\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}\right)(w)
			:= {}&
			\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{0},1-\kappa}
			\big(
			\Sigma(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
			\big)(w)
			+\mu\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\gamma_{1},2}
			\left(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}\right)(w)
			+\textcolor{red}{G^{s}}(u_0)(w).
		\end{align*}	
		Using an analogous argument to that in the proof of Proposition~\ref{onwall}
		(cf.~\hyperref[Step10]{\textbf{Step 1}}), we deduce that, for every
		$r\in[s,s+T)$ and $w\in O^{r}_{s+T}$,
		\begin{align}\label{CZ1}
			\begin{split}
				\left(\mathbf{R}^+_r\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}\right)(w)
				={}&
				\mathcal{G}^{r}_{\gamma_{0},1-\kappa}
				\big(
				\Sigma(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
				\big)(w)
				+\mu\mathcal{G}^{r}_{\gamma_{1},2}
				\left(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}\right)(w)
				+\textcolor{red}{G^{r}}
				\big(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(r,\cdot)\big)(w),
				\\
				&\mathcal{R}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(r,x)
				=\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(r,x),
				\quad
				\text{with the convention that, if } r=s,\;
				\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(s,\cdot)=u_0(\cdot).
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		Thus, \begin{align}\label{CZ22} 
			\left(\mathbf{R}^+_r\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}\right)(w) = \mathcal{Q}_{s+T-r}^{r} \left( \mathcal{R}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(r,\cdot), \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0} \right)(w). 
		\end{align}
		Consider
		\begin{align}\label{BBBBB}
			r\in [s,s+T)
			\quad \text{and} \quad
			0<\delta\leq s+T-r.
		\end{align}
		With these preparations, we divide the rest of the proof into several steps.
		
		\textbf{Step 1.}\label{Step1111}
		In this step, for $a\in \mathcal{A}^-=\{\textcolor{red}{\bullet},  
	\mathbin{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.35ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.4em$\textcolor{red}{\mathord{\rotatebox[origin=c]{60}{$/$}}}$ 
				\kern-0.45em\raisebox{-1.ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
, \large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}\textcolor{red}{\bullet}, i=1,\cdots, d\}$, we focus on estimating
		\begin{align*}
			\sup_{\substack{
					z,w\in O_{\delta+r}^{r},\ z\neq w\\
					d(z,w)\leq\Vert z,w\Vert_{P_r}
			}}
			\frac{
				\Vert z,w\Vert_{P_r}^{\gamma_1}
				\,
				\Big\Vert
				\mathrm{Pr}_{a}\Big[
				\big(\Sigma(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0})\tilde{\star}\textcolor{red}{\bullet}\big)(z)
				-\Gamma_{z,w}
				\big(
				\Sigma(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0})\tilde{\star}\textcolor{red}{\bullet}
				\big)(w)
				\Big]
				\Big\Vert
			}{
				d(z,w)^{\gamma_0-|a|_h}
			}.
		\end{align*}
		\textbf{Case $a=\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$}: Let
		\begin{align}\label{Bnassssw}
			\begin{split}
				&\eta_{1}(z,w):=-\Sigma(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w))\Pi_z( 
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
)(w),\\
				&\eta_{2}(z,w):=-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}_{u_0}(w)	\Pi_z(\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}})(w).
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		Thus
		\begin{align}\label{NMasz}
			\mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}\big[
			\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\left(
			\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(w)
			\right)\big]
			=
			\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(z)-\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)-\eta_1(z,w)-\eta_2(z,w).
		\end{align}
		By \eqref{B1478} and \eqref{Hnasssss}, we have
		\begin{align}
			\begin{split}
				&\mathrm{Pr}_{\textcolor{red}{\bullet}}\big[
				\big(\Sigma(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
				\big)(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(
				\left(
				\Sigma(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
				\right)(w)
				\big)\big]\\&=\Sigma(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(z))-\Sigma(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w))+\Sigma^{\prime}(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w))\Sigma(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w))\Pi_z( 
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
)(w) +\sum_{i=1}^{d}\Sigma^{\prime}(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w))\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}_{u_0}(w)	\Pi_z(\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}})(w)\\&=\underbrace{\Sigma(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(z))-\Sigma\big(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)+\eta_1(z,w)+\eta_2(z,w)\big)}_{\text{I}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0},z,w)}\\
				&+\underbrace{\Sigma\big(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)+\eta_1(z,w)+\eta_2(z,w)\big)-\Sigma\big(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)+\eta_2(z,w)\big)-\Sigma^{\prime}\big(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)\big)\eta_1(z,w)}_{\text{II}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0},z,w)}\\&+\underbrace{\Sigma\big(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)+\eta_2(z,w)\big)-\Sigma(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w))-\Sigma^{\prime}(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w))\eta_2(z,w)}_{\text{III}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0},z,w)}.
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		From the Taylor expansion, it is clear that
		\begin{enumerate}
			\item
			\begin{align*}
				&\text{I}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0},z,w) \\
				&= \int_{0}^{1}
				\Sigma'\bigg(
				\alpha \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(z)
				+ (1-\alpha)\big(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)+\eta_1(z,w)+\eta_2(z,w)\big)
				\bigg) 
				\mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}\big[
				\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(w)\big)
				\big]\,\mathrm{d}\alpha.
			\end{align*}
			\item 
			\begin{align*}
				& \text{II}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0},z,w) \\
				&= \int_0^1\int_0^1
				\Sigma''\big(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)+\beta\eta_2(z,w)+\alpha\beta\eta_1(z,w)\big)
				\big[\eta_2(z,w)+\alpha\eta_1(z,w)\big]\eta_1(z,w)
				\,\mathrm{d}\beta\,\mathrm{d}\alpha.
			\end{align*}
			
			\item 
			\begin{align*}
				\text{III}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0},z,w)
				&=
				\int_{0}^{1}
				\big[
				\Sigma^{\prime}\big(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)+\alpha\eta_{2}(z,w)\big)
				-
				\Sigma^{\prime}\big(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)\big)
				\big]
				\eta_{2}(z,w)\,\mathrm{d}\alpha
				\\
				&=
				\int_0^1\int_0^1
				\alpha\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\big(
				\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)+\alpha\beta\eta_2(z,w)
				\big)
				\,\,(\eta_2(z,w))^2
				\,\mathrm{d}\beta\,\mathrm{d}\alpha.
			\end{align*}
			Thanks to the assumptions on $\Sigma$ and a simple interpolation argument, we obtain that
			\begin{align*}
				\Vert \text{III}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0},z,w)\Vert
				\lesssim
				\Vert\eta_{2}(z,w)\Vert^{\gamma_1}.
			\end{align*}
		\end{enumerate}
		Using these latter expressions together with the assumptions on $\Sigma$, we conclude that
		\begin{align}\label{PLPLP}
			\begin{split}
				&\big\Vert \mathrm{Pr}_{\textcolor{red}{\bullet}}\big[
				\big(\Sigma(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
				\big)(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(
				\left(
				\Sigma(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
				\right)(w)
				\big)\big]\big\Vert \lesssim \big\Vert \mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}\big[
				\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(
				\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(w)
				\big)\big]\big\Vert\\&+\Vert \Pi_z( 
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
)(w)\Vert^{2}+d(z,w) \Vert \Pi_z( 
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
)(w)\Vert\sum_{i=1}^{d}\Vert\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}_{u_0}(w)\Vert+ d(z,w)^{\gamma_1}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\Vert\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}_{u_0}(w)\Vert^{\gamma_1}.
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		Since $\gamma_1<2-2\kappa$ and, for
		$z,w\in O_{\delta+r}^{\,r}$, we have $\|z,w\|_{P_r}\leq \delta^{\frac12},$ it follows from \eqref{PLPLP} that
		\begin{align}\label{A11}
			\begin{split}
				&\sup_{\substack{ z,w \in O_{\delta+r}^{r},\ z\neq w\\ d(z,w)\leq\Vert z,w\Vert_{P_r}}}
				\frac{\Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{r}}^{\gamma_1} \, \big\Vert \mathrm{Pr}_{\textcolor{red}{\bullet}}\big[
					\big(\Sigma(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
					\big)(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(
					\left(
					\Sigma(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
					\right)(w)
					\big)\big]\big\Vert}{d(z,w)^{\gamma_1}}\\&\lesssim
				\sup_{\substack{ z,w \in O_{\delta+r}^{r},\ z\neq w\\ d(z,w)\leq\Vert z,w\Vert_{P_r}}}
				\frac{\Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{r}}^{\gamma_1}\big\Vert \mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}\big[
					\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(
					\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(w)
					\big)\big]\big\Vert}{d(z,w)^{\gamma_1}}+\delta^{1-\kappa}\big(
				[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
\ ]_{\overline{{O}_{\delta+r}^r}}\big)^2\\&+\delta^{\frac{1-\kappa}{2}}[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
\ ]_{\overline{{O}_{\delta+r}^r}}\sup_{w\in O^{r}_{\delta+r}}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\Vert w\Vert_{P_{r}}\Vert\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}_{u_0}(w)\Vert+\big(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sup_{w\in O^{r}_{\delta+r}}\Vert w\Vert_{P_{r}}\Vert\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}_{u_0}(w)\big)^{\gamma_1}
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		\textbf{Case} $a= 
	\mathbin{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.35ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.4em$\textcolor{red}{\mathord{\rotatebox[origin=c]{60}{$/$}}}$ 
				\kern-0.45em\raisebox{-1.ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
$:
		By definition,
		\begin{align}\label{ZZXZX}
			\begin{split}
				&\mathrm{Pr}_{ 
	\mathbin{

				\raisebox{1.35ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$}

	} 
}\big[
				\big(\Sigma(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
				\big)(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(
				\left(
				\Sigma(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
				\right)(w)
				\big)\big]\\&= \Sigma^{\prime}(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(z))\Big[\Sigma(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(z))-\Sigma(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w))\Big]+\Sigma(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w))\Big[\Sigma^{\prime}(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(z))-\Sigma^{\prime}(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w))\Big].
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		Let $\widetilde{\Sigma}$ denote either $\Sigma$ or $\Sigma'$. In either case, we have
		\begin{align}\label{LLMOLPa}
			\begin{split}
				&\widetilde{\Sigma}(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(z))
				-\widetilde{\Sigma}(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w))
				\\
				&=
				\int_{0}^{1}
				\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\prime}\big(
				\alpha\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(z)
				+(1-\alpha)\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)
				\big)
				\mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}\big[
				\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(
				\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(w)
				\big)
				\big]
				\,\mathrm{d}\alpha
				\\
				&\quad+
				\int_{0}^{1}
				\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\prime}\big(
				\alpha\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(z)
				+(1-\alpha)\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)
				\big)
				\big[
				\eta_1(z,w)+\eta_2(z,w)
				\big]
				\,\mathrm{d}\alpha .
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		Therefore, from the assumptions on $\Sigma$ and since $\gamma_1<2-2\kappa$, we conclude from \eqref{ZZXZX} that
		\begin{align}\label{A33}
			\begin{split}
				&\sup_{\substack{
						z,w \in O_{\delta+r}^{r},\ z\neq w\\
						d(z,w)\leq\Vert z,w\Vert_{P_r}}}
				\frac{
					\Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{r}}^{\gamma_1}
					\big\Vert\mathrm{Pr}_{

	} 
}\big[

				\\&+\delta^{1-\kappa}[\

	} 
 \ ]_{\overline{{O}_{\delta+r}^r}}
				+\delta^{\frac{1-\kappa}{2}}

		\textbf{Case $a=\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$}: By definition,
		\begin{align*}
			&\mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}\textcolor{red}{\bullet}}\big[

			-

		Also,
		\begin{align}\label{BBNMNa}

				\Sigma^{\prime}\big(
				\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)+\eta_1(z,w)+\eta_2(z,w)

				-

				\big\Vert
				\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}_{u_0}(z)

		We now estimate the terms on the right-hand side of \eqref{BBNMNa} as follows:
		\begin{enumerate}
			\item
			For the first term, using the identity
			$AB=(AB^{\frac{1}{\gamma_1}})B^{\frac{\gamma_1-1}{\gamma_1}}$
			and the assumptions on $\Sigma$, we obtain
			\begin{align*}

				-

				-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(
				\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(w)
				\big)

				\big\Vert^{\frac{\gamma_1-1}{\gamma_1}}.
			\end{align*}
			
			\item
			For the second term, using the boundedness of $\Sigma$, $\Sigma^{\prime}$, and $\Sigma^{\prime\prime}$, we have
			\begin{align*}

				\big\Vert
				\\
				&\lesssim
				\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}_{u_0}(z)\Vert
				\Vert\Pi_z(

	} 
)(w)\Vert .

			\item
			For the third term, using the boundedness of $\Sigma$, $\Sigma^{\prime}$, and $\Sigma^{\prime\prime}$ together with an interpolation argument yields
			\begin{align*}

			\item

				\big\Vert

				&\lesssim

				\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}_{u_0}(z)
				-
				\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}_{u_0}(w)
				\Vert .

		\end{enumerate}
		Putting these estimates together, and keeping in mind that $\gamma_1<2-2\kappa$, we conclude that
		\begin{align}\label{NNNNN}
			\begin{split}
				&\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sup_{\substack{
						z,w \in O_{\delta+r}^{r},\ z\neq w\\
						d(z,w)\leq\Vert z,w\Vert_{P_r}}}
				\frac{\Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{r}}^{\gamma_1}\big\Vert \mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}\textcolor{red}{\bullet}}\big[
					\big(\Sigma(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}
					\big)(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(

					\right)(w)
					\big)\big]\big\Vert}{d(z,w)^{\gamma_1-1}}\\&\lesssim \Big[\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sup_{w\in O_{\delta+r}^r } \Vert w\Vert_{P_r}\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}(w)\Vert\Big]\bigg[\sup_{\substack{
						z,w \in O_{\delta+r}^{r},\ z\neq w\\
						d(z,w)\leq\Vert z,w\Vert_{P_r}}}
				\frac{\Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{r}}^{\gamma_1}\big\Vert \mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}\big[
					\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(
					\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(w)
					\big)\big]\big\Vert}{d(z,w)^{\gamma_1}}\bigg]^{\frac{\gamma_1-1}{\gamma_1}}\\&+ \delta^{\frac{1-\kappa}{2}}[\  
	\mathrel{

				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ ]_{{O}_{\delta+r}^r}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sup_{w\in O_{\delta+r}^{r} } \Vert w\Vert_{P_r}\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}(w)\Vert\Big]+\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sup_{w\in {O}_{\delta+r}^r} \Vert w\Vert_{P_r}\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}(w)\Vert\Big]^{\gamma_1}\\&+\sum_{i=1}^d\sup_{\substack{
						z,w \in O_{\delta+r}^{r},\ z\neq w\\

		By using the inequality
		\begin{align*}
			AB^{\frac{\gamma_1-1}{\gamma_1}}\lesssim A^{\gamma_1}+B,

		it follows from \eqref{NNNNN} that

						z,w \in O_{\delta+r}^{r},\ z\neq w\\
						d(z,w)\leq\Vert z,w\Vert_{P_r}}}
				\frac{\Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{r}}^{\gamma_1}\big\Vert \mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}\big[
					\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(
					\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(w)
					\big)\big]\big\Vert}{d(z,w)^{\gamma_1}}\\&+\delta^{\frac{1-\kappa}{2}}[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ ]_{{O}_{\delta+r}^r}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sup_{w\in O_{\delta+r}^{r} } \Vert w\Vert_{P_r}\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}(w)\Vert\Big]+\sum_{i=1}^d\sup_{\substack{
						z,w \in O_{\delta+r}^{r},\ z\neq w\\
						d(z,w)\leq\Vert z,w\Vert_{P_r}}}

					\big\Vert \mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}\big[

		\textbf{Step 2.}\label{Step2222}  Now we establish a clean estimate for
		\[
		\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_r\left(\Sigma(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}) \tilde{\star} \textcolor{red}{\bullet}\right) \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_0,-1-\kappa,O_{\delta+r}^r}^{(r)}.
		\]
		First,	we now proceed to estimate
		\begin{align*}
			\left(
			\sum_{i=1}^{d}
			\sup_{w\in O_{\delta+r}^r}
			\Vert w\Vert_{P_r}\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}(w)\Vert
			\right)^{\gamma_1}.
		\end{align*}
		For $w=(t,x)\in O_{\delta+r}^{r}$, we set
		\begin{align}\label{TTyyta}
			\begin{split}
				\mathcal{P}_{1}(w,\delta+r)
				&=
				\sup_{\substack{
						z \in O_{\delta+r}^r\\
						d(w,z)\leq\frac{\Vert w\Vert_{P_r}}{2}
				}}
				\big\Vert 
				\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(z)
				-\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)
				-\eta_1(z,w)
				\big\Vert ,
				\\
				\mathcal{P}_{2}(w,\delta+r)
				&=
				\sup_{\substack{
						z\in O_{\delta+r}^r,\ z\neq w\\
						d(w,z)\leq\frac{\Vert w\Vert_{P_r}}{2}
				}}
				\frac{
					\big\Vert 
					\mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}\big[
					\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}
					\big(
					\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0}(w)
					\big)
					\big]\big\Vert
				}{
					d(z,w)^{\gamma_1}
				},
				\\
				R(w,\delta)
				&=
				\min\bigg\{
				\frac{\Vert w\Vert_{P_r}}{2},
				\left[
				\frac{\mathcal{P}_{1}(w,\delta+r)}
				{\mathcal{P}_{2}(w,\delta+r)}
				\right]^{\frac{1}{\gamma_1}}
				\bigg\}\footnotemark.
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		\footnotetext{We assume that
			$\mathcal{P}_{1}(w,\delta+r)$ and $\mathcal{P}_{2}(w,\delta+r)$ are non-zero.
			The degenerate cases lead to the same final estimate.}
		For each $1\leq j\leq d$, we choose
		$\tilde{w}_{j}=(t,\tilde{x})\in O_{\delta+r}^{\,r}$ such that
		\begin{align*}
			X^{k}(\tilde{w}_{j})=
			\begin{cases}
				X^{j}(w)-R(w,\delta)\dfrac{\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^j$}}}}_{u_0}(w)}
				{\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^j$}}}}_{u_0}(w)\Vert}\footnotemark,
				& k=j,
				\\[6pt]
				X^{k}(w),
				& \text{otherwise}.
			\end{cases}
		\end{align*}
		\footnotetext{We assume that
			$\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^j$}}}}_{u_0}(w)\Vert \neq 0$.
			Otherwise, the final estimate is trivial.}	
		Thus,
		\[
		d(w,\tilde{w}_j)=R(w,\delta)\leq \frac{\Vert w\Vert_{P_r}}{2}.
		\]
		
		From \eqref{Bnassssw}, we obtain
		\[
		-\eta_2(\tilde{w}_{j},w)=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}_{u_0}(w)\Pi_{\tilde{w}_j}(\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}})(w)
		=
		R(w,\delta)\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^j$}}}}_{u_0}(w)\Vert.
		\]
		Therefore, together with \eqref{NMasz}, and \eqref{TTyyta}, we conclude that
		\begin{align*}
			\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^j$}}}}_{u_0}(w)\Vert
			\leq
			\mathcal{P}_{2}(w,\delta+r)\,R(w,\delta)^{\gamma_1-1}
			+
			\frac{\mathcal{P}_{1}(w,\delta+r)}{R(w,\delta)}.
		\end{align*}
		and hence, by substituting the definition of $R(w,\delta)$, we obtain:
		\begin{align*}
			&\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^j$}}}}_{u_0}(w)\Vert
			\\&\leq
			\big[\mathcal{P}_{2}(w,\delta+r)\big]^{\frac{1}{\gamma_1}}
			\big[\mathcal{P}_{1}(w,\delta+r)\big]^{\frac{\gamma_1-1}{\gamma_1}}
			+
			\max\bigg\{
			\big[\mathcal{P}_{2}(w,\delta+r)\big]^{\frac{1}{\gamma_1}}
			\big[\mathcal{P}_{1}(w,\delta+r)\big]^{\frac{\gamma_1-1}{\gamma_1}},
			\frac{2\mathcal{P}_{1}(w,\delta+r)}{\Vert w\Vert_{P_r}}
			\bigg\}.
		\end{align*}
		Thus
		\begin{align*}
			&	\Vert w\Vert_{P_r}\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^j$}}}}_{u_0}(w)\Vert
			\leq

		Consequently

		Note that for $a\in \mathcal{A}^-=\{\textcolor{red}{\bullet},

, \large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}\textcolor{red}{\bullet},\, i=1,\ldots,d\}$, we have

		\textbf{Step 3.}\label{Step3333} We now finalize the proof. Recall that \eqref{CZ1} and \eqref{CZ22} hold. By \eqref{A-1}, \eqref{fixed-point}, and \eqref{A6}, one can find a constant $C\geq 1$ such that

				\|\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)\|\Big)^{\gamma_1-1}\right]\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_r\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{\delta+r}^r}^{(r)}\\&+M_{3}(\mathcal{Z},r,\delta+r)\Big(\sup_{w\in O_{\delta+r}^r}
				\|\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)\|\Big)^{\gamma_1},

		We aim to apply Lemma~\ref{global}. Let $b < c$ be points in $[s, s+T]$ such that $c-b \leq \delta_\beta$. By definition

		Let $\beta=\frac{1}{2}$. Then
		\begin{enumerate}
			\item If $\delta_{\frac{1}{2}}<T$, then from \eqref{NMaz}, \eqref{ASSferg} and Lemma~\ref{global}, we obtain
			\begin{align}\label{SSAS78sasas}
				\begin{split}
					&\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{s+T}}^{(s)}
					\lesssim
					\left(\frac{1}{\delta_{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)^{\frac{\gamma_1+2}{2}}
					\big( \|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+T}^s}}\big)^2
					\Lambda(\frac{1}{2},u_0,\mathcal{Z})\\
					&\lesssim P_{1}\Big(\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{s+1+T}^{s-1}}},[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
\ ]_{\overline{O_{s+T}^s}}, \|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+T}^s}},\sup_{w\in O_{s+T}^s}
					\|\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)\|,\|u_0\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}\Big)
				\end{split}
			\end{align}
			where
			\begin{align*}
				P_{1}(b,c,d,f,g):=&
				d^2
				(1+b^2)^{\frac{\gamma_1+2}{1-\kappa}}
				\big(1+|\mu|+c+f^{\gamma_1-1}\big)^{\frac{\gamma_1+2}{1-\kappa}}
				\\
				&\times
				\big(
				f+g+(1+b^2)(1+c^2+f^{\gamma_1})
				\big).
			\end{align*}
			\item  If $\delta_{\frac{1}{2}}=T$, then from \eqref{ASSferg},
			\begin{align*}
				&\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^+_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}_{u_0} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{s+T}}^{(s)}\lesssim \tilde{P}_{1}\Big(\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\overline{O_{s+1+T}^{s-1}}},[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
\ ]_{\overline{O_{s+T}^s}}, \|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+T}^s}},\sup_{w\in O_{s+T}^s}
				\|\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}_{u_0}(w)\|,\|u_0\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^d)}\Big)
			\end{align*}
			where
			\begin{align*}
				\tilde{P}_{1}(b,c,d,f,g)=\big(f+g+(1+b^2)(1+c^2+f^{\gamma_{1}})\big).
			\end{align*}
		\end{enumerate}
		Since $ \|\Gamma\|_{\overline{O_{s+T}^s}}\geq 1$, the bound obtained in \eqref{SSAS78sasas} is also valid when $\delta_{\frac{1}{2}}=T$. This finishes the proof.
<!-- end proof-35 -->
<!-- proof-36: body_md -->
\label{AAzza1}
		We estimate each of the terms appearing in
		\[
		\lvert\!\lVert 
			\mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}F\big(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}\big)
			-
			\mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}F\big(\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}\big)
		 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^{s}}^{(s)}
		\]
		in \eqref{Q00}. Since the proof is somewhat lengthy, we divide it into several
		steps. We first introduce the following notation.
		\begin{align*}
			&\eta_1(z,w):=-\Sigma(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w))\Pi_z( 
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
)(w), \qquad
			\eta_2(z,w):=-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}(w)\Pi_z(\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}})(w), \\
			&\widetilde{\eta}_{1}(z,w):=-\Sigma(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w))\Pi_z( 
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
)(w), \qquad
			\widetilde{\eta}_{2}(z,w):=-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}(w)\Pi_z(\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}})(w),
		\end{align*}
		for all $z,w\in O_{T+s}^{s}$ such that $d(z,w)\leq \Vert z,w\Vert_{P_s}$. Also, throughout the proof, we repeatedly use the following elementary identity without further mention:
		\begin{align*}
			\prod_{i=1}^{m} A_i - \prod_{i=1}^{m} B_i
			= \sum_{j=1}^{m}
			\big(\prod_{i=1}^{j-1} B_i\big)
			(A_j - B_j)
			\big(\prod_{k=j+1}^{m} A_k\big),
			\qquad A_i,B_i\in\mathbb{R}, \ \ \prod_{i=1}^{0}(\cdot)=\prod_{i=m+1}^{m}(\cdot)=1.
		\end{align*}
		Also, $M_{\nu}(b) := \max\{b, b^{\nu}\}$.
		
		\textbf{Step 1.}\label{Step11}
		In this step, we estimate
		\begin{align*}
			\sup_{\substack{z,w \in O_{T+s}^{s}, z\neq w\\ d(z,w)\leq \Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}}}
			\frac{
				\Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1}
				\, \big\Vert \mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}\big[
				\big(F(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})-F(\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}})\big)(z)
				-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(\big(F(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})-F(\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}})\big)(w)\big)
				\big]\big\Vert
			}{
				d(z,w)^{\gamma_1}
			}.
		\end{align*}
		This is the most involved term in
		$\lvert\!\lVert 
			\mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}F\big(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}\big)
			-
			\mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}F\big(\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}\big)
		 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^{s}}^{(s)}$.
		Note that
		\begin{align}\label{NASBs}
			\begin{split}
				&z,w \in O_{T+s}^{s}, \quad d(z,w)\leq \Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}:\\
				&\Vert\eta_1(z,w)-\widetilde{\eta}_{1}(z,w)\Vert
				\leq \Vert \Pi_z( 
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
)(w)\Vert\,
				\Vert \Sigma(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)) - \Sigma(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w))\Vert \\
				&\qquad \lesssim d(z,w)^{1-\kappa}[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ ]_{\overline{{O}_{T+s}^s}}\,
				\lvert\!\lVert  \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} - \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}  \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)},\\[0.5em]
				&\Vert\eta_2(z,w)-\widetilde{\eta}_{2}(z,w)\Vert
				\leq \sum_{i=1}^{d}\Vert \Pi_z(\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}})(w)\Vert\,
				\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}(w)-\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}(w)\Vert \\
				&\qquad \leq \sum_{i=1}^{d}d(z,w)\,
				\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}(w)-\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$X^i$}}}}(w)\Vert \lesssim\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s} \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} - \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}  \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}.
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		From \eqref{B1478}, \eqref{Hnasssss}, we have :
		\begin{align*}
			&\mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}\big[
			F(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(
			F(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})(w)
			\big)\big] \\
			&= \underbrace{F(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(z))
				- F\big(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)+\eta_1(z,w)+\eta_2(z,w)\big)}_{\text{I}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}},z,w)} \\
			&\quad + \underbrace{
				F\big(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)+\eta_1(z,w)+\eta_2(z,w)\big)
				- F\big(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)+\eta_2(z,w)\big)
				- F^{\prime}\big(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)\big)\eta_1(z,w)
			}_{\text{II}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}},z,w)} \\
			&\quad + \underbrace{
				F\big(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)+\eta_2(z,w)\big)
				- F(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w))
				- F^{\prime}(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w))\eta_2(z,w)
			}_{\text{III}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}},z,w)}
		\end{align*}
		Using this expression, we conclude the following items:
		\begin{itemize}
			\item[1)] By a simple Taylor expansion, we have:
			\begin{align*}
				&\text{I}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}},z,w) \\
				&= \int_{0}^{1}
				F'\bigg(
				\alpha \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(z)
				+ (1-\alpha)\big(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)+\eta_1(z,w)+\eta_2(z,w)\big)
				\bigg) 
				\mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}\big[
				\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w)\big)
				\big]\,\mathrm{d}\alpha.
			\end{align*}
			Together with the assumptions on $F$ and $\Sigma$, we obtain that
			\begin{align*}
				& \Vert\text{I}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}},z,w)-\text{I}(\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}},z,w)\Vert	
				\lesssim \big\Vert \mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}\big[
				(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}-\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}})(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(
				(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}-\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}})(w)
				\big)\big]\big\Vert+\\&\big(\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(z)-\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(z)\Vert+\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)-\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)\Vert+\Vert\eta_1(z,w)-\widetilde{\eta}_{1}(z,w)\Vert+\Vert\eta_2(z,w)-\widetilde{\eta}_{2}(z,w)\Vert \big)\\
				&\times \big\Vert \mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}\big[
				\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(
				\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w)
				\big)\big]\big\Vert.
			\end{align*}
			Therefore, from \eqref{NASBs}, we conclude that
			\begin{align*}
				&\sup_{\substack{z,w \in O_{T+s}^{s},z\neq w\\d(z,w)\leq \Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}}} \frac{\Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1} \Vert\text{I}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}},z,w)-\text{I}(\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}},z,w)\Vert }{d(z,w)^{\gamma_1}}	
				\\&\lesssim   \lvert\!\lVert   \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} -\mathbf{R}^{+}_{s} \textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}  \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}\big[\big(1+[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ ]_{\overline{{O}_{T+s}^s}}\big)\lvert\!\lVert  \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}+1 \big]
			\end{align*}
			\item[2)]  Again, a direct consequence of the Taylor expansion yields
			\begin{align*}
				& \text{II}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}},z,w) \\
				&= \int_0^1\int_0^1
				F''\big(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)+\beta\eta_2(z,w)+\alpha\beta\eta_1(z,w)\big)
				\big[\eta_2(z,w)+\alpha\eta_1(z,w)\big]\eta_1(z,w)
				\,\mathrm{d}\beta\,\mathrm{d}\alpha.
			\end{align*}
			Thus, under the assumptions on $F$ and $\Sigma$, we obtain
			\begin{align*}
				&\Vert \text{II}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}},z,w) -\text{II}(\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}},z,w) \Vert\\
				&\lesssim \big(\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)-\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)\Vert+\Vert\eta_1(z,w)-\widetilde{\eta}_{1}(z,w)\Vert+\Vert\eta_2(z,w)-\widetilde{\eta}_{2}(z,w)\Vert \big)^{\nu}\\&\times\big(\Vert \eta_1(z,w)\Vert^2+\Vert \eta_1(z,w)\Vert \Vert \eta_2(z,w)\Vert\big)\\&+\big(\Vert\eta_1(z,w)-\widetilde{\eta}_{1}(z,w)\Vert+\Vert\eta_2(z,w)-\widetilde{\eta}_{2}(z,w)\Vert\big)\Vert\eta_1(z,w)\Vert\\&+\big(\Vert \widetilde{\eta}_{1}(z,w)\Vert+\Vert \widetilde{\eta}_{2}(z,w)\Vert\big)\Vert\eta_1(z,w)-\widetilde{\eta}_{1}(z,w)\Vert.
			\end{align*}
			Consequently, from \eqref{NASBs}, it follows that
			\begin{align*}
				&\sup_{\substack{z,w \in O_{T+s}^{s},z\neq w\\d(z,w)\leq \Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}}} \frac{\Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1} \Vert\text{II}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}},z,w)-\text{II}(\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}},z,w)\Vert }{d(z,w)^{\gamma_1}}	
				\\&\lesssim   \big(\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s} \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} - \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}  \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}\big)^{\nu}\big(1+[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ ]_{\overline{{O}_{T+s}^s}}\big)^\nu[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ ]_{\overline{{O}_{T+s}^s}}\bigg(\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s} \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}  \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}+[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ ]_{\overline{{O}_{T+s}^s}}\bigg)\\&+\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s} \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} - \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}  \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ ]_{\overline{{O}_{T+s}^s}}\bigg(1+[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ ]_{\overline{{O}_{T+s}^s}}+\lvert\!\lVert  \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}  \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}\bigg)\\&\lesssim M_{\nu}(\lvert\!\lVert  \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} - \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}  \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)})\\&\times[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ ]_{\overline{{O}_{T+s}^s}}\big(1+[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ ]_{\overline{{O}_{T+s}^s}}\big)^\nu\bigg(1+[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ ]_{\overline{{O}_{T+s}^s}}+\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s} \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}  \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}+\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s} \textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}  \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}\bigg).
			\end{align*}
			\item[3)] Analogously to the previous items, from the Taylor expansion we have
			\begin{align*}
				&\text{III}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}},z,w)= \int_0^1\int_0^1
				F^{\prime\prime}\big(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)+\alpha\beta\eta_2(z,w)\big)
				\,\alpha\,(\eta_2(z,w))^2
				\,\mathrm{d}\beta\,\mathrm{d}\alpha.
			\end{align*}
			Then
			\begin{align*}
				&\Vert \text{III}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}},z,w) -\text{III}(\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}},z,w) \Vert\\
				&\lesssim \big(\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)-\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)\Vert+\Vert\eta_2(z,w)-\widetilde{\eta}_{2}(z,w)\Vert \big)^{\nu}\Vert \eta_2(z,w)\Vert^2\\&+\big(\Vert {\eta}_{2}(z,w)\Vert+\Vert \widetilde{\eta}_{2}(z,w)\Vert\big)\Vert\eta_2(z,w)-\widetilde{\eta}_{2}(z,w)\Vert.
			\end{align*}
			Consequently, from \eqref{NASBs},
			\begin{align*}
				&\sup_{\substack{z,w \in O_{T+s}^{s}, z\neq w\\d(z,w)\leq \Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}}} \frac{\Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1} \Vert\text{III}(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}},z,w)-\text{III}(\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}},z,w)\Vert }{d(z,w)^{\gamma_1}}\\&\lesssim M_{\nu}(\lvert\!\lVert  \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} -\mathbf{R}^{+}_{s} \textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}  \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)})\bigg[\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s} \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}  \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}+\big(\lvert\!\lVert  \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}  \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}\big)^2+\lvert\!\lVert  \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}  \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}\bigg].	
			\end{align*}
		\end{itemize}
		Finally, combining these three items, we conclude that
		\begin{align}\label{Z11}
			\begin{split}
				&
				\frac{
					\Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1}
					\, \big\Vert \mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}\big[
					\big(F(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})-F(\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}})\big)(z)
					-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(\big(F(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})-F(\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}})\big)(w)\big)
					\big]\big\Vert
				}{
					d(z,w)^{\gamma_1}
				}
				\\
				&\lesssim
				M_{\nu}\!\left(
				\lvert\!\lVert   \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} -\mathbf{R}^{+}_{s} \textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}  \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}
				\right)
				P\big(
				[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ ]_{\overline{{O}_{T+s}^s}},
				\lvert\!\lVert \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s} \textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}  \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)},
				\lvert\!\lVert  \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}  \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}
				\big),
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		where
		\begin{align*}
			P(b,c,d)=\max\left\lbrace (1+b)c+1, b(1+b)^\nu(1+b+c+d),c+c^2+d\right\rbrace.
		\end{align*}
		\textbf{Step 2.}\label{Step22}
		Let us now estimate
		\begin{align*}
			\sup_{\substack{z,w \in O_{T+s}^{s}, z\neq w\\ d(z,w)\leq \Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}}}
			\frac{
				\Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1}
				\, \big\Vert \mathrm{Pr}_{ 
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
}\big[
				\big(F(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})-F(\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}})\big)(z)
				-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(\big(F(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})-F(\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}})\big)(w)\big)
				\big]\big\Vert
			}{
				d(z,w)^{\gamma_1-1+\kappa}
			}.
		\end{align*}
		The method of proof is similar to that of the previous step. From \eqref{B1478}, \eqref{Hnasssss}, by a direct use of the Taylor expansion, we have
		\begin{align}\label{Aza41}
			\begin{split}
				&\mathrm{Pr}_{ 
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
}\big[
				F(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(F(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})(w)\big)
				\big]
				=F^\prime(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w))\big[\Sigma(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(z))-\Sigma(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w))\big]+\Sigma(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(z))\big[F^\prime(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(z))-F^\prime(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w))\big]\\
				&=
				F^{\prime}(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w))
				\int_{0}^{1}
				\Sigma'\big(
				\alpha \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(z)
				+(1-\alpha)\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)
				\big)
				\mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}\big[
				\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w)\big)
				\big]
				\,\mathrm{d}\alpha
				\\
				&\quad+
				F^{\prime}(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w))
				\int_{0}^{1}
				\Sigma'\big(
				\alpha \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(z)
				+(1-\alpha)\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)
				\big)
				\big[\eta_1(z,w)+\eta_2(z,w)\big]
				\,\mathrm{d}\alpha
				\\
				&\quad+
				\Sigma(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(z))
				\int_{0}^{1}
				F^{\prime\prime}\big(
				\alpha \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(z)
				+(1-\alpha)\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)
				\big)
				\mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}\big[
				\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w)\big)
				\big]
				\,\mathrm{d}\alpha
				\\
				&\quad+
				\Sigma(\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(z))
				\int_{0}^{1}
				F^{\prime\prime}\big(
				\alpha \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(z)
				+(1-\alpha)\mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)
				\big)
				\big[\eta_1(z,w)+\eta_2(z,w)\big]
				\,\mathrm{d}\alpha .
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		
		Recalling the assumptions on $F$ and $\Sigma$, we then derive
		\begin{align}\label{LLm}
			\begin{split}
				&\big\Vert \mathrm{Pr}_{ 
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{ 
				\raisebox{1.5ex}{$\textcolor{red}{\bullet}$} 
				\kern-0.9ex
				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
}\big[
				\big(F(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})-F(\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}})\big)(z)
				-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(\big(F(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})-F(\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}})\big)(w)\big)
				\big]\big\Vert
				\\
				&\lesssim
				M_{\nu}\big(
				\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(z)-\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(z)\Vert
				+\Vert \mathcal{U}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)-\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}(w)\Vert
				\big)
				\\
				&\qquad\times
				\bigg[
				\big\Vert\mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}\big[
				\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}(w)\big)
				\big]\big\Vert
				+\Vert\eta_1(z,w)\Vert
				+\Vert\eta_2(z,w)\Vert
				\bigg]

				&\quad+
				\bigg[
				\big\Vert \mathrm{Pr}_{\large{\text{\color{red}{$1$}}}}\big[
				(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}-\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}})(z)

				\Vert\eta_1(z,w)-\widetilde{\eta}_{1}(z,w)\Vert
				+\Vert\eta_2(z,w)-\widetilde{\eta}_{2}(z,w)\Vert
				\bigg].
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		Keeping in mind that $\gamma_1 < 2-2\kappa$ and using \eqref{NASBs}, we deduce that
		\begin{align}\label{LLMM}
			\begin{split}
				&z,w \in O_{T+s}^{s}, z\neq w , \quad d(z,w)\leq \Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}:

				}
				\lesssim
				\Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1}
				d(z,w)^{2-2\kappa-\gamma_1}
				[\  
	\mathrel{ 
		\vcenter{ 
			\hbox{

				\raisebox{0.6ex}{\smash{\textcolor{red}{$\vert$}}} 
			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ ]_{\overline{{O}_{T+s}^s}}
				\lvert\!\lVert 
					\mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}
					-\mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}
				 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}
				\\
				&\qquad\lesssim
				[\  
	\mathrel{

			} 
		} 
	} 
 \ ]_{\overline{{O}_{T+s}^s}}\,
				\lvert\!\lVert 
					\mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}}
					-\mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}
				 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)},
				\\[0.5em]
				&
				\frac{
					\Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1}
					\Vert\eta_2(z,w)-\widetilde{\eta}_{2}(z,w)\Vert
				}{
					d(z,w)^{\gamma_1-1+\kappa}

				\lesssim

				&\qquad\lesssim

				 \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}.
			\end{split}
		\end{align}
		Thus, from \eqref{LLm} and \eqref{LLMM}, we deduce that
		\begin{align}\label{Z22}
			\begin{split}
				&\sup_{\substack{z,w \in O_{T+s}^{s},z\neq w\\d(z,w)\leq \Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}}} \frac{	\Vert z,w \Vert_{P_{s}}^{\gamma_1}\big\Vert 	\mathrm{Pr}_{

	} 
}\big[
					\big(F(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})-F(\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}})\big)(z)-\Gamma_{z,w}\big(\big(F(\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}})-F(\textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}})\big)(w)\big)
					\big]\big\Vert }{d(z,w)^{\gamma_1-1+\kappa}}\\&\lesssim M_{\nu}\big(\lvert\!\lVert   \mathbf{R}^{+}_{s}\textcolor{red}{\mathcal{U}} -\mathbf{R}^{+}_{s} \textcolor{red}{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}  \rVert\!\rvert_{\gamma_1,0,O_{T+s}^s}^{(s)}\big)\big[1+[\  
	\mathrel{
